Door muggen overdraagbare virussen: een toenemende bedreiging voor de volksgezondheid

Authors: K. TONG-MINH, W.D. BOON VON OCHSSÉE
Original abstract (English)
Mosquito-borne viruses: an increasing risk for European travelers and public health Mosquito-borne viral infections pose an increasing threat to public health, amplified by globalization and the advance of invasive mosquito species such as Aedes albopictus. International travel exposes travelers to a range of viruses, while endemic areas grapple with significant disease burden and socio-economic consequences. Japanese encephalitis, chikungunya, Zika, and dengue illustrate the diversity of this threat. This article is a narrative review based on the recent literature and epidemiological data. Japanese encephalitis, transmitted by Culex mosquitoes, causes severe neurological complications, particularly in Asia. Chikungunya, spread by Aedes mosquitoes, is characterized by prolonged joint pain. Zika, also transmitted by Aedes, is notorious for congenital abnormalities. Dengue, with 4 serotypes, has a global distribution and can lead to severe hemorrhagic fever. For none of these viral infections is there a specific antiviral treatment; the approach is mainly symptomatic. Climate change and the further spread of invasive mosquito species are expected to lead to an increase in outbreaks, both in frequency and geographical spread. Continuous monitoring, vector control, public education, and vaccination efforts are essential to address this growing threat.
View review five-element analysis
Scope and question
The review focuses on mosquito-borne viruses as an increasing threat to public health, particularly for European travelers, with emphasis on dengue virus among other viruses like Japanese encephalitis, chikungunya, and Zika.
Evidence base and method
This is a narrative review based on recent literature and epidemiological data, without specifying databases, search dates, or study counts.
Consensus and conclusions
Dengue virus, with 4 serotypes, has a global distribution and can lead to severe hemorrhagic fever; no specific antiviral treatments exist, and management is primarily symptomatic.
Controversies and gaps
The review does not report specific controversies or evidence gaps, but notes the increasing threat due to climate change and invasive mosquito species.
Research and practice implications
Continuous monitoring, vector control, public education, and vaccination efforts are essential to address the growing threat of mosquito-borne viruses, including dengue.

媒介传播疾病控制的技术创新及其与公共卫生的关系

Technological Innovations in Vector-Borne Disease Control and Their Relationship with Public Health
作者： Fairul Kader, Astri Ivo, Manli Desri Helden, Ainun Muthmainnah
摘要中文翻译背景:媒介 传播的疾病仍然是印度尼西亚公共卫生的重大挑战。持续使用化学杀虫剂导致了抗药性,而气候变化和媒介行为则进一步加剧了媒介疾病。因此,需要创新、有效和可持续的技术来支持基于社区的 媒介 控制工作。目的:根据过去五年的文献,回顾 媒介 传播疾病控制中的各种技术创新及其与改善公共卫生的关系。方法:本研究是采用系统和叙述方法的文献综述。该搜索是通过 PubMed、Scopus、Google Scholar、Garuda 和 Indonesia OneSearch 数据库进行的,使用关键词“媒介 控制技术创新”和“公共卫生”。纳入的文章是 2020 年至 2025 年与该主题相关的出版物。结果:从所选的 20 篇文章中,确定了五项主要创新:沃尔巴克氏体技术、昆虫不育技术 (SIT) 和基因驱动、使用吡丙醚的自动传播、利用当地资源的植物性生物杀虫剂以及基于物联网和地理信息系统的数字系统。这些创新已被证明可以降低登革热和疟疾等疾病的 媒介 密度和 发病率,并增强社区参与。结论:与传统方法相比,创新技术已被证明更加环保和高效。它们的成功实施需要政策支持、基础设施准备以及综合 媒介 管理 (IVM) 框架内的跨部门协作。关键词:技术创新、媒介 控制、公共卫生、沃尔巴克氏体、自传播。
查看综述五要素
范围与问题
该综述的重点是 登革病毒 及其与公共卫生的关系,特别审查了 媒介 传播疾病控制中可以改善登革热治疗结果的技术创新。核心问题是这些创新对于基于社区的 媒介 控制工作是否有效且可持续。
证据基础与方法
该综述是通过 PubMed、Scopus、Google Scholar、Garuda 和 Indonesia OneSearch 数据库进行的文献分析,使用与 媒介 控制技术和公共卫生相关的关键词。它包含 2020 年至 2025 年发表的文章,采用系统性和叙述性的方法。
共识与结论
该审查确定了 媒介 控制方面的五项主要技术创新,这些创新已被证明可以降低登革热和其他相关疾病的 媒介 密度和 媒介 密度。其中包括沃尔巴克氏体技术、昆虫不育技术 (SIT) 和基因驱动、使用吡丙醚的自动传播、植物性生物杀幼虫剂以及基于物联网和地理信息系统的数字系统。这些创新被认为比传统方法更环保、更高效。
争议与缺口
审查指出,虽然这些创新显示出希望,但其实施需要政策支持、基础设施准备和跨部门合作。需要进一步研究来应对扩展这些技术并确保其在登革热控制方面的长期有效性的挑战。
科研与实践启示
审查强调了政策支持、基础设施发展和跨部门合作对于成功实施这些技术的重要性。它还表明需要进行更多研究来评估这些创新在不同环境和人群中的有效性。

Technological Innovations in Vector-Borne Disease Control and Their Relationship with Public Health

Authors: Fairul Kader, Astri Ivo, Manli Desri Helden, Ainun Muthmainnah
Original abstract (English)
Background: Diseases transmitted by vectors remain a major challenge to public health in Indonesia. Continuous use of chemical insecticides has led to resistance, while climate change and vector behavior further exacerbate disease transmission. Therefore, innovative, effective, and sustainable technologies are needed to support community-based vector control efforts. Objective: To review various technological innovations in vector-borne disease control and their relationship with improving public health based on literature from the past five years. Methods: This study is a literature review employing a systematic and narrative approach. The search was conducted through the PubMed, Scopus, Google Scholar, Garuda, and Indonesia OneSearch databases using the keywords “vector control technology innovation” and “public health.” Articles included were publications from 2020–2025 that were relevant to the topic. Results: From the 20 selected articles, five major innovations were identified: the Wolbachia technique, Sterile Insect Technique (SIT) and gene drive, autodissemination using pyriproxyfen, plant-based biolarvicides utilizing local resources, and digital systems based on IoT and GIS. These innovations have been shown to reduce vector density and the incidence of diseases such as dengue and malaria, as well as enhance community participation. Conclusion: Innovative technologies have proven to be more environmentally friendly and efficient compared to conventional methods. Their successful implementation requires policy support, infrastructure readiness, and cross-sector collaboration within the framework of Integrated Vector Management (IVM). Keywords: Technological innovation, vector control, public health, Wolbachia, autodissemination.
View review five-element analysis
Scope and question
The review focuses on dengue virus and its relationship with public health, specifically examining technological innovations in vector-borne disease control that can improve outcomes for dengue. The central question is whether these innovations are effective and sustainable for community-based vector control efforts.
Evidence base and method
The review is a literature analysis conducted through PubMed, Scopus, Google Scholar, Garuda, and Indonesia OneSearch databases, using keywords related to vector control technology and public health. It includes articles published from 2020 to 2025 and employs a systematic and narrative approach.
Consensus and conclusions
The review identifies five major technological innovations in vector control that have been shown to reduce vector density and the incidence of dengue and other related diseases. These include the Wolbachia technique, Sterile Insect Technique (SIT) and gene drive, autodissemination using pyriproxyfen, plant-based biolarvicides, and digital systems based on IoT and GIS. These innovations are considered more environmentally friendly and efficient than conventional methods.
Controversies and gaps
The review notes that while these innovations show promise, their implementation requires policy support, infrastructure readiness, and cross-sector collaboration. There is a need for further research to address the challenges of scaling these technologies and ensuring their long-term effectiveness in dengue control.
Research and practice implications
The review highlights the importance of policy support, infrastructure development, and cross-sector collaboration for the successful implementation of these technologies. It also suggests the need for more studies to evaluate the effectiveness of these innovations in different settings and populations.

太平洋地区的登革热:多国情况(截至 2026 年 7 月 20 日) - ReliefWeb

Dengue in the Pacific: Multicountry Situation (As of 20 July 2026) - ReliefWeb
新闻简报太平洋共同体 (SPC) 和世界卫生组织 (WHO) 于 2026 年 7 月 20 日发布了一份有关太平洋岛屿登革热活动的情况报告。自2025年初以来,已报告了12起登革热暴发,导致21,258例确诊病例和21人死亡。2026年,新喀里多尼亚、汤加和瓦努阿图谢法省正式宣布暴发。2026年1月至7月的数据显示,确诊病例4,447例,住院治疗172例,死亡3例。虽然库克群岛已经结束了 暴发,但瓦努阿图的 传播 仍在继续,而包括美属萨摩亚和萨摩亚在内的其他几个国家仍然存在低水平的 传播。监测 系统注意到,与前一年相比,登革热样疾病的总体趋势有所下降,目前流行的是 DENV-1 和 DENV-2 血清型。
查看新闻五要素
时间
报告于2026年7月20日发布,涵盖2025年1月1日至2026年7月2日的数据。
地点与对象
太平洋岛屿国家和地区 (PIC),特别是美属萨摩亚、库克群岛、斐济、法属波利尼西亚、基里巴斯、瑙鲁、新喀里多尼亚、萨摩亚、汤加、图瓦卢、瓦努阿图(谢法省)以及瓦利斯和富图纳。
事件
SPC 和 WHO 发布的多国情况报告详细介绍了太平洋地区的登革热 暴发。2025年,报告了12起暴发;2026年,新喀里多尼亚、汤加和瓦努阿图谢法省正式宣布暴发。库克群岛于 2026 年 5 月 25 日宣布其 暴发 结束。
规模、影响与风险
2025年,太平洋地区有21,258例确诊病例,21人死亡。2026年1月1日至2026年7月2日期间,共有4,447例确诊病例,172例住院治疗,3例死亡。太平洋综合症 监测 系统报告了 8,644 例登革热样疾病病例,趋势低于上一年。DENV-1 和 DENV-2 正在流通。
应对、状态与不确定性
截至 2026 年 7 月 20 日,暴发 仍在瓦努阿图谢法省持续进行,DENV-1 正在传播。美属萨摩亚、基里巴斯、新喀里多尼亚、萨摩亚和汤加观察到 传播 水平较低。由于最近社区 传播 的报告,瓦利斯和富图纳群岛正受到监控。
A high priorityPublic-health news

Dengue in the Pacific: Multicountry Situation (As of 20 July 2026) - ReliefWeb

News Brief
The Pacific Community (SPC) and the World Health Organization (WHO) released a situation report on 20 July 2026 regarding dengue activity across the Pacific Islands. Since the start of 2025, 12 dengue outbreaks have been reported, resulting in 21,258 confirmed cases and 21 fatalities. In 2026, outbreaks were officially declared in New Caledonia, Tonga, and Shefa province, Vanuatu. Data from January to July 2026 indicates 4,447 confirmed cases, 172 hospitalizations, and 3 deaths. While the Cook Islands has ended its outbreak, transmission continues in Vanuatu, and low levels of transmission persist in several other nations including American Samoa and Samoa. Surveillance systems note a lower overall trend of dengue-like illness compared to the previous year, with DENV-1 and DENV-2 serotypes currently circulating.

QDENGA是什么?随着病例持续上升,印度出现首例登革热病毒 疫苗;了解可用性、好处和 - 免费新闻杂志

What Is QDENGA? India Gets Its First Dengue Vaccine As Cases Continue To Rise; Know Availability, Benefits And - Free Press Journal
新闻简报印度药品监管机构中央药品标准控制组织 (CDSCO) 已批准 QDENGA (TAK-003),这是该国首个治疗登革热的 疫苗。由武田开发的减毒活 疫苗 旨在预防所有四种 登革病毒 血清型(DENV-1、DENV-2、DENV-3 和 DENV-4)。它分两剂注射,间隔三个月,其优点是不需要事先进行登革热感染血液检测。此次批准之际,印度正面临着沉重的疾病负担,城市化和气候变化导致病例不断增加。虽然 疫苗 预计将显着减少住院治疗和严重疾病,但其在诊所的立即可用性将取决于供应和分销物流。卫生当局尚未最终确定符合资格年龄组的具体指南。
查看新闻五要素
时间
发布于美国标准时间 2026 年 7 月 20 日星期一下午 02:03。
地点与对象
印度;特别提到疫苗为国内首个登革热批准文号。
事件
中央药品标准控制组织 (CDSCO) 批准武田的 QDENGA (TAK-003) 疫苗,标志着印度第一个获得许可的登革热 疫苗,旨在预防所有四种 登革病毒 血清型。
规模、影响与风险
印度是世界上登革热负担最重的国家之一,过去二十年登革热病例急剧增加;疫苗 预计将降低住院风险和 重症登革热,从而减轻医疗保健系统的压力。
应对、状态与不确定性
尽管已获得监管部门批准,但 疫苗 可能无法立即在医院和诊所使用,因为商业启动取决于制造、供应、定价和分销计划;卫生当局预计将发布有关符合资格的年龄组和 疫苗接种 指南的详细建议。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

What Is QDENGA? India Gets Its First Dengue Vaccine As Cases Continue To Rise; Know Availability, Benefits And - Free Press Journal

News Brief
India's drug regulator, the Central Drugs Standard Control Organisation (CDSCO), has approved QDENGA (TAK-003), the country's first vaccine for dengue. Developed by Takeda, the live attenuated vaccine is designed to protect against all four dengue virus serotypes (DENV-1, DENV-2, DENV-3, and DENV-4). Administered in two doses three months apart, it offers the advantage of not requiring prior blood testing for dengue infection. The approval comes as India faces a high burden of the disease, with cases rising due to urbanization and climate change. While the vaccine is expected to significantly reduce hospitalizations and severe disease, its immediate availability in clinics will depend on supply and distribution logistics. Health authorities are yet to finalize specific guidelines on eligible age groups.

Sunanda SinghUpdated: Monday, July 20, 2026, 02:03 PM IST What Is QDENGA? | India has taken a major step in the fight against dengue after the Central Drugs Standard Control Organisation (CDSCO) approved Takeda's QDENGA (TAK-003). The country's first dengue vaccine offers a major step forward in the fight against one of the country's most widespread mosquito-borne diseases. Developed by Japanese pharmaceutical company Takeda, the vaccine has already been approved in several countries and is expected to help reduce the burden of severe dengue infections. Let us know! 👂What type of content would you like to see from us this year?— HubSpot (@HubSpot) What is QDENGA?India has done something significant against dengue and its approval came after India continues to shoulder one of the world's highest dengue burdens, with cases increasing sharply over the past two decades due to urbanisation, climate change, changing mosquito habitats and population growth. But what exactly is QDENGA?QDENGA, also known as TAK-003, is a live, attenuated dengue vaccine designed to protect against all four dengue virus serotypes (DENV-1, DENV-2, DENV-3 and DENV-4). It is administered as two doses, with the second dose given three months after the first. Benefits of the vaccineClinical studies have shown that QDENGA helps reduce the risk of hospitalisation and severe dengue, which can lead to complications such as dengue haemorrhagic fever and dengue shock syndrome. While no vaccine provides 100% protection, it is expected to significantly lower the severity of the disease and reduce pressure on healthcare systems during dengue outbreaks. Unlike the older dengue vaccines, QDENGA does not require people to undergo blood testing to determine whether they had previously dengue.Availability in IndiaThe vaccine has already been approved in more than 40 countries, including the European Union, the United Kingdom, Brazil, Indonesia and Argentina. India now joins that list at a time when dengue is emerging as one of the country's biggest seasonal public health threats.Although the vaccine has received regulatory approval in India, it may not be immediately available across hospitals and clinics. Its commercial launch will depend on manufacturing, supply, pricing and distribution plans. Health authorities are also expected to issue detailed recommendations regarding eligible age groups and vaccination guidelines. 随着南亚病例激增,印度批准首个登革热 疫苗

India approves first dengue vaccine as cases surge in South Asia
新闻简报印度已批准其首个登革热药物 疫苗(QDENGA),该药物由日本武田药品工业株式会社(Takeda Pharmaceutical)开发,目前南亚正在应对感染激增的问题。中央药物标准控制组织在审查临床试验数据后,授权 疫苗 用于成人和 4 至 60 岁的儿童。这一决定是在地区卫生系统面临巨大压力之际作出的。斯里兰卡今年报告了超过 76,000 例病例和 53 例死亡,而孟加拉国正在与一场重大的 暴发 作斗争,有超过 9,000 例病例和 30 人死亡。武田估计,印度承担着全球登革热负担的近三分之一。虽然 疫苗 被视为公共卫生措施的重要补充,但监管机构要求进行上市后研究,专家指出它应该补充而不是取代 媒介 控制和 监测。
查看新闻五要素
时间
发布于 2026 年 7 月 20 日。该报告引用了周日报告的斯里兰卡近期登革热病例数以及今年的累计总数。
地点与对象
主要事件涉及印度,特别涉及国家药品监管机构 CDSCO。该报告还提到了更广泛的南亚地区,特别是斯里兰卡和孟加拉国,暴发 正在影响着那里的普通民众。
事件
印度药品监管机构药品标准控制中心中心 (CDSCO) 已批准武田制药的登革热 疫苗 QDENGA。此次批准是在对全球和印度 III 期试验数据进行审查之后获得的。该事件已确认。
规模、影响与风险
此次批准正值南亚登革热病例激增之际。斯里兰卡单日报告新增病例 2,652 例,使全年病例总数达到 76,044 例,其中至少 53 人死亡。孟加拉国正面临严重的 暴发,已有 9,000 多例病例和 30 人死亡,给卫生系统带来了更大的压力。武田估计印度占全球登革热负担的近三分之一。
应对、状态与不确定性
监管机构得出的结论是,疫苗 对于 4 至 60 岁的成人和儿童来说是安全的且具有免疫原性。CDSCO 要求在商业上市后六个月内进行上市后安全性和有效性研究。专家强调,疫苗接种 应补充 监测 和 媒介 控制等现有策略,并指出孕妇和新生儿等特定人群不会从 疫苗 中受益。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

India approves first dengue vaccine as cases surge in South Asia

News Brief
India has approved its first dengue vaccine, QDENGA, developed by Japan's Takeda Pharmaceutical, as South Asia grapples with a surge in infections. The Central Drugs Standard Control Organisation authorized the vaccine for adults and children aged four to 60 following a review of clinical trial data. This decision comes as regional health systems face significant strain; Sri Lanka has reported over 76,000 cases and 53 deaths this year, while Bangladesh is battling a major outbreak with more than 9,000 cases and 30 fatalities. Takeda estimates that India bears nearly one-third of the global dengue burden. While the vaccine is seen as a critical addition to public health measures, regulators have required post-marketing studies, and experts note it should complement, not replace, vector control and surveillance.
Original source text (English)
India has approved its first dengue vaccine as cases of the mosquito-borne disease surge in South Asia. Japan's Takeda Pharmaceutical's dengue vaccine QDENGA has been approved by India's drug regulator, the Centre Drugs Standard Control Organisation (CDSCO). The global threat posed by dengue has been rising for years, as climate change, urbanisation and international travel aid the spread of the mosquitoes that transmit it. The approval comes as hospitals across South Asia grapple with a surge in dengue infections. In Sri Lanka, health authorities reported 2,652 new cases on Sunday alone, taking the total to 76,044 dengue cases so far this year. At least 53 people have died. Bangladesh is also suffering a major outbreak with over 9,000 cases and 30 deaths that has piled pressure on its health system even as it battles a measles epidemic . Dengue is transmitted to humans through the bites of infected Aedes mosquitoes. Its symptoms include fever, severe muscle pain, and in severe cases, shock and internal bleeding that can be fatal. Although mortality is relatively low, outbreaks place a substantial burden on health systems. Takeda's QDENGA jab is a live-attenuated tetravalent vaccine designed to protect against all four dengue virus serotypes. It can be given regardless of previous dengue infections. "The approval comes at a critical time for public health, as reported dengue cases in India have increased 11-fold in the past two decades," the company said in a statement. Dengue patients receive treatment at the District General Hospital in Sri Lanka's Negombo city - ISHARA S. KODIKARA/AFP via Getty Images Takeda estimates that India accounts for almost one-third of the global dengue burden. The approval follows a review of global and Indian phase III trial data involving more than 20,000 participants across eight countries, including Sri Lanka, as well as 480 volunteers in India. Regulators concluded the vaccine was safe and immunogenic in adults and children aged between four and 60 years. However, the CDSCO directed the company to undertake post-marketing safety and effectiveness studies in India within six months of the vaccine's commercial launch. QDENGA has already been approved in more than 40 countries and is WHO pre-qualified. In 2024, Takeda partnered with Indian vaccine manufacturer Biological E to manufacture up to 50 million doses annually, supporting Takeda's target of producing 100 million doses globally each year by the end of the decade. Dr Eng Eong, a dengue expert and professor in Emerging Infectious Diseases at Duke-NUS in Singapore, told the Telegraph that it was critical for India and other dengue-affected countries to move beyond strategies reliant on vector-control. "A licenced dengue vaccine [in India] adds an important piece to the very limited public health arsenal to combat dengue," he said. Shikha Taneja Malik, Senior Scientific Affairs Manager, Drugs for Neglected Diseases initiative, South Asia, said: "A locally manufactured, affordable vaccine could meaningfully reduce severe cases and hospital strain. But there are still specific populations such as pregnant women, newborns, and older adults who would not benefit from this vaccine." Ms Malik said the vaccination should complement surveillance, vector control and development of new treatments to help reduce the burden of the disease. Security Council that “critical minerals are increasingly viewed as strategic assets, not just commodities, and a major source of interstate competition.” Major economies are racing to get supplies of the minerals needed for technology from smartphones to fighter jets to use as leverage, she said. 新抗体有望缓解 登革热 - 台北时报

New antibody offers possible dengue fever relief - Taipei Times
新闻简报Taiwanese researchers have developed a novel bispecific antibody that shows promise as a treatment for severe dengue fever. The team from the National Health Research Institutes and National Cheng Kung University identified that the severe inflammatory response in dengue is driven by two immune receptors, CLEC5A and TLR2. By simultaneously blocking both receptors, their antibody effectively suppresses the excessive inflammatory response caused by all four major dengue virus serotypes. Globally, dengue affects an estimated 390 million people annually, with 96 million developing symptoms and 22,000 dying. Taiwan experienced its largest outbreak in decades in 2015, with 43,000 cases and 228 deaths. The research, published in the Journal of Biomedical Science, offers a potential new therapeutic approach for a disease with no effective vaccine and limited treatment options.
查看新闻五要素
时间
发表日期:2026-07-20。事件日期:未明确报告,但研究成果于今年7月(2026年7月)发表,并于昨日(2026年7月21日)举行新闻发布会。
地点与对象
Taiwan, with global context. Research team from National Health Research Institutes (NHRI) and National Cheng Kung University (NCKU). Affected population: 390 million dengue virus infections worldwide annually, with 96 million symptomatic cases and 500,000 severe cases leading to 22,000 deaths. Taiwan-specific: 43,000 confirmed cases and 228 deaths during the 2015 DENV-2 outbreak.
事件
王仁仁教授和谢世良研究员领导的研究小组确定了登革病毒感染中严重炎症反应的机制,并开发了一种双特异性抗体来阻断它。该抗体通过抑制所有四种主要 登革病毒 血清型引起的过度炎症反应,显示出作为 重症登革热 发烧的 治疗 的前景。该研究发表在本月的《生物医学科学杂志》上。
规模、影响与风险
Global scale: 390 million dengue infections annually, 96 million symptomatic cases, 500,000 severe cases, 22,000 deaths. Taiwan-specific: 2015 outbreak had 43,000 confirmed cases and 228 deaths. Risk: Dengue fever remains a difficult-to-control public health challenge. No effective vaccine exists, and treatment relies on supportive care. The condition can lead to dengue hemorrhagic fever or dengue shock syndrome.
应对、状态与不确定性
研究现状:双特异性抗体已开发出来并进行了实验测试,显示出对所有四种主要血清型和一些 突变 连锁菌株引起的 重症登革热 的有效性。该研究已发表,但可能需要进一步的开发和临床试验。公共卫生应对:嘉义市政府卫生局确认一宗输入性登革热病例,并实施灭蚊措施(喷洒、调查、清除孳生地)。来源限制:本文是完整的报告,但摘要是基于提供的证据。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

New antibody offers possible dengue fever relief - Taipei Times

News Brief
Taiwanese researchers have developed a novel bispecific antibody that shows promise as a treatment for severe dengue fever. The team from the National Health Research Institutes and National Cheng Kung University identified that the severe inflammatory response in dengue is driven by two immune receptors, CLEC5A and TLR2. By simultaneously blocking both receptors, their antibody effectively suppresses the excessive inflammatory response caused by all four major dengue virus serotypes. Globally, dengue affects an estimated 390 million people annually, with 96 million developing symptoms and 22,000 dying. Taiwan experienced its largest outbreak in decades in 2015, with 43,000 cases and 228 deaths. The research, published in the Journal of Biomedical Science, offers a potential new therapeutic approach for a disease with no effective vaccine and limited treatment options.
Original source text (English)
Front Page Taiwan News Business Editorial & Opinion Sports World News Features Bilingual Pages SearchSearch All Front Page Taiwan News Business Editorial & Opinion Sports World News Features Bilingual Pages June 23, 2026 - July 22, 2026 GO Home Taiwan News Tue, Jul 21, 2026 page4 New antibody offers possible dengue fever relief By Lee I-chia / Staff reporter Add TT as Preferred Source A research team from the National Health Research Institutes (NHRI) and National Cheng Kung University (NCKU) has identified the mechanism behind the severe inflammatory response triggered by dengue virus infection and developed a bispecific antibody that blocks it, showing promise as a treatment for dengue fever. The research team was led by NCKU Department of Medical Laboratory Science and Biotechnology professor Wang Jen-ren (王貞仁) and NHRI Immunology Research Center Distinguished Investigator Hsieh Shie-liang (謝世良). The team said an estimated 390 million dengue virus infections occur worldwide each year, with about 96 million people developing symptoms and approximately 500,000 progressing to severe disease, resulting in about 22,000 deaths annually. National Health Research Institutes president Sytwu Huey-kang, second from right, poses for a photograph with other experts during a news conference in the Ministry of Health and Welfare in Taipei yesterday. Photo: CNA In 2015, a DENV-2 dengue fever outbreak in Taiwan resulted in 43,000 confirmed cases and 228 deaths, the country’s largest dengue outbreak in decades. NHRI President Sytwu Huey-kang (司徒惠康) said at a news conference yesterday at the Ministry of Health and Welfare that dengue fever remains a public health challenge that is difficult to control. Patients might initially experience only a mild fever, but it is often followed by symptoms such as pain behind the eyes and joint pain, he said. In some cases, the condition can lead to dengue hemorrhagic fever or dengue shock syndrome. There is no vaccine to effectively prevent dengue fever, and clinical treatment relies mainly on supportive care, Hsieh said. During dengue infection, activation of immune cells drives excessive production of pro-inflammatory factors, contributing to fever, he said. The pathogenesis of severe dengue is widely believed to involve dengue virus-induced overactivation of immune cells, which triggers a cytokine storm. The excessive release of cytokines increases vascular permeability, leading to plasma leakage, dengue shock syndrome and organ dysfunction, he added. Hsieh said their study confirmed that the severe inflammatory response triggered by dengue virus infection is not driven by a single immune receptor, but by the combined action of two immune receptors.The team demonstrated that simultaneously blocking CLEC5A and TLR2 can effectively suppress the excessive inflammatory response caused by dengue virus. They designed and developed a bispecific antibody capable of targeting both receptors at the same time, he said. Wang said experiments showed that the bispecific antibody reduced various inflammatory responses associated with severe dengue caused by all four major dengue virus serotypes, as well as some strains carrying mutations linked to severe disease. The team said the antibody could be a novel treatment for severe dengue fever and might have applications against Zika virus and Japanese encephalitis. The NHRI said the research findings were published this month in the Journal of Biomedical Science. Separately, the Chiayi City Government Health Bureau yesterday confirmed the city’s first imported dengue fever case, involving a student who had returned from Vietnam. The student traveled to Vietnam during summer vacation and developed fever, headache and pain behind the eyes last Wednesday before testing positive for dengue fever on Saturday, the bureau said. The bureau sprayed the area within a 100m of the victim’s home, increased mosquito density surveys and removed possible breeding sites. It advised residents to eliminate standing water and take precautions against mosquito bites, especially during dengue season. Affected population: 390 million dengue virus infections worldwide annually, with 96 million symptomatic cases and 500,000 severe cases leading to 22,000 deaths. Taiwan-specific: 43,000 confirmed cases and 228 deaths during the 2015 DENV-2 outbreak.
Event
A research team led by Professor Wang Jen-ren and Distinguished Investigator Hsieh Shie-liang identified the mechanism behind severe inflammatory response in dengue virus infection and developed a bispecific antibody that blocks it. The antibody showed promise as a treatment for severe dengue fever by suppressing excessive inflammatory responses caused by all four major dengue virus serotypes. B 常规重要公共卫生新闻

印度首个登革热 疫苗 获批:DCGI 批准武田 Qdenga 用于 4 至 60 岁人群 - 印度快报

India’s first dengue vaccine approved: DCGI clears Takeda’s Qdenga for those aged 4 to 60 - The Indian Express
新闻简报India’s drug regulator has approved Takeda’s Qdenga, the first dengue vaccine available in the country, for individuals aged 4 to 60. The two-dose regimen protects against all four dengue virus serotypes and does not require pre-vaccination testing. The approval is supported by clinical trials involving over 28,000 participants, showing 80.2% efficacy against virologically confirmed dengue and high protection against hospitalization. This development comes as India faces a significant dengue burden, with reported cases rising 11-fold in two decades and the country accounting for nearly one-third of global cases. Manufacturing partner Biological E will produce up to 50 million doses annually, though pricing remains undisclosed.
查看新闻五要素
时间
印度药品监管总局 (DCGI) 于 2026 年 7 月 20 日批准了 疫苗,文章于 2026 年 7 月 21 日更新。此次批准是在长期临床试验(包括 4.5 年的随访数据)之后进行的。
地点与对象
疫苗 批准用于 4 至 60 岁的个人。临床试验涉及全球 28,000 多名参与者,还有一项针对印度参与者的特定 III 期试验。
事件
DCGI 批准了武田的 Qdenga (TAK-003),使其成为印度第一个登革热 疫苗。该批准基于武田的全球临床开发计划和在印度进行的 III 期试验。疫苗 旨在预防所有四种 登革病毒 血清型(DENV-1、DENV-2、DENV-3、DENV-4)。
规模、影响与风险
过去 20 年来,印度报告的登革热病例增加了 11 倍,到 2025 年将报告 113,000 例病例。印度占全球登革热负担的近三分之一。疫苗 在 12 个月时对经病毒学证实的登革热的总体疗效显示为 80.2%,在 18 个月时对登革热相关住院治疗的总体疗效为 90.4%。
应对、状态与不确定性
The vaccine is administered as a two-dose regimen (0.5 mL each) given three months apart. It does not require pre-vaccination testing. Manufacturing partner Biological E can produce up to 50 million doses annually. Pricing has not been announced but is expected to be competitive. The vaccine has WHO prequalification.

India’s first dengue vaccine approved: DCGI clears Takeda’s Qdenga for those aged 4 to 60 - The Indian Express

News Brief
India’s drug regulator has approved Takeda’s Qdenga, the first dengue vaccine available in the country, for individuals aged 4 to 60. The two-dose regimen protects against all four dengue virus serotypes and does not require pre-vaccination testing. The approval is supported by clinical trials involving over 28,000 participants, showing 80.2% efficacy against virologically confirmed dengue and high protection against hospitalization. This development comes as India faces a significant dengue burden, with reported cases rising 11-fold in two decades and the country accounting for nearly one-third of global cases. Manufacturing partner Biological E will produce up to 50 million doses annually, though pricing remains undisclosed.
Original source text (English)
India’s first dengue vaccine approved: DCGI clears Takeda’s Qdenga for those aged 4 to 60 The two-dose vaccine protects against all four dengue virus strains without requiring pre-vaccination testing, as India battles a rising dengue burden. Written by: Anuradha Mascarenhas 4 min read Pune Updated: Jul 21, 2026 04:51 AM IST The approval comes at a critical time for public health, with reported dengue cases in India increasing 11-fold over the past two decades. (Image generated using AI) The Drug Controller General of India (DCGI) has approved Japanese company Takeda Biopharmaceuticals India’s dengue vaccine, Qdenga, making it the first such vaccine to be greenlighted in India. It is designed to protect against all four dengue virus strains (DENV-1, DENV-2, DENV-3 and DENV-4) and can be administered regardless of prior dengue exposure. It does not require pre-vaccination testing, the company said. The vaccine is administered subcutaneously. It comes as a two-dose regimen of 0.5 mL each, with doses given three months apart. The price has not been announced yet but since Takeda has entered into a manufacturing partnership with Hyderabad -based Biological E to produce up to 50 million doses annually, costs are expected to be competitive for the Indian market. Pricing will also depend on whether this gets incorporated into government immunisation programmes later. Also Read | Dengue is spreading fast: Can cutting-edge science outsmart it at last? The approval comes at a critical time for public health, with reported dengue cases in India increasing 11-fold over the past two decades. India continues to account for nearly one-third of the global dengue burden. Dengue is a mosquito-borne viral disease that poses a significant global public health threat, with prevalence in over 125 countries. India accounts for nearly one-third of the global dengue burden. In an official statement issued today, Dr Mahender Nayak, head of Intercontinental Markets, Takeda, said, “Dengue is a growing public health challenge and India needs sustained, evidence-based prevention.” The latest seven-year data for Qdenga show continued protection against dengue infection and hospitalisation across all four serotypes — an important milestone for communities and health systems.” Since its launch in 2022, Qdenga has been approved in 43 countries, with more than 32 million doses distributed globally. Dr Goh Choo Beng, Medical Affairs Head, Southeast Asia and India Cluster, said, “India carries a substantial dengue burden, and all four dengue virus serotypes have been documented as co-circulating in several regions. The approval represents an important step in strengthening India’s comprehensive approach to dengue prevention, alongside vector control, surveillance, community awareness and other public health measures.” Also Read | Serum Institute of India ties up with DNDi for Phase III trial of dengue therapy What is the vaccine about? It is designed to protect against all four dengue virus serotypes. It is approved for use in the four to 60 year age group, regardless of previous dengue exposure, without the need for pre-vaccination testing.It is the most extensively studied dengue vaccine, with long-term clinical data demonstrating sustained protection against dengue disease and dengue-related hospitalisation. Story continues below. Subscribe to see fewer ads. What did clinical trials show? The approval in India is based on results from Takeda’s global clinical development programme, involving 19 Phase 1, 2 and 3 clinical trials with over 28,000 participants in both endemic and non-endemic regions. This included the pivotal Phase III TIDES (DEN-301) trial, which evaluated the vaccine in over 20,000 participants across eight countries over an extended period to evaluate both the long-term safety and efficacy of QDENGA. At 12 months following the second dose, the study met its primary endpoint, demonstrating an overall vaccine efficacy of 80.2% against virologically confirmed dengue (VCD). At 18 months, the study also demonstrated 90.4% efficacy against dengue-related hospitalisation, meeting a key secondary endpoint of the study. After 4.5 years, two doses of QDENGA provided 84.1% vaccine efficacy in preventing dengue-related hospitalisations. Notably, the vaccine has also shown sustained safety and efficacy for up to seven years across all four dengue virus serotypes. This approval is also supported by data from a Phase III clinical trial (DEN-302) conducted in Indian participants, which evaluated the safety and immunogenicity of the vaccine in the 4–60 age group. The study demonstrated that QDENGA was found to be tolerated, safe and immunogenic in healthy adults, adolescents and children. These clinical outcomes are further supported by real-world evidence generation and ongoing pharmacovigilance to deepen understanding of the vaccine’s safety and impact. WHO nod The vaccine has also received WHO prequalification, confirming it meets global standards for quality, safety and efficacy, and enabling eligibility for procurement through international agencies such as UNICEF and PAHO to support wider access. Story continues below. Subscribe to see fewer ads. In 2025, reported cases exceeded 113,000, but modelling analyses suggest the dengue burden in India appears to be higher than reported, likely representing tens of millions of infections each year. © The Indian Express Pvt Ltd Anuradha Mascarenhas Anuradha Mascarenhas is a Senior Editor at The Indian Express, based in Pune. View five news elements
Time
The Drug Controller General of India (DCGI) approved the vaccine on July 20, 2026, with the article updated on July 21, 2026. The approval follows clinical trials conducted over an extended period, including 4.5 years of follow-up data.
Location and population
The vaccine is approved for individuals aged 4 to 60 years. Clinical trials involved over 28,000 participants globally and a specific Phase III trial in Indian participants.
Event
The DCGI approved Takeda’s Qdenga (TAK-003), making it India’s first dengue vaccine. The approval is based on Takeda’s global clinical development programme and a Phase III trial in India. The vaccine is designed to protect against all four dengue virus serotypes (DENV-1, DENV-2, DENV-3, DENV-4).
Scale, impact and risk
Reported dengue cases in India have increased 11-fold over the past two decades, with 113,000 cases reported in 2025. India accounts for nearly one-third of the global dengue burden. The vaccine demonstrated 80.2% overall efficacy against virologically confirmed dengue at 12 months and 90.4% efficacy against dengue-related hospitalization at 18 months.
Response, status and uncertainty
The vaccine is administered as a two-dose regimen (0.5 mL each) given three months apart. It does not require pre-vaccination testing. Manufacturing partner Biological E can produce up to 50 million doses annually. Pricing has not been announced but is expected to be competitive. The vaccine has WHO prequalification.

B 常规重要公共卫生新闻

DCGI 批准武田 (Takeda) 的 QDENGA,印度首个登革热 疫苗:内部详细信息 - WION

DCGI clears Takeda's QDENGA, India's first-ever dengue vaccine: Full details inside - WION
新闻简报The Drug Controller General of India (DCGI) has approved QDENGA, developed by Takeda Biopharmaceuticals India, as the country's first dengue vaccine. The vaccine is approved for individuals aged four to 60 and offers protection against all four dengue virus serotypes without requiring pre-vaccination testing. It is administered in a two-dose regimen given three months apart. Clinical trials, including a pivotal Phase III study involving over 20,000 participants, showed 80.2% efficacy against virologically confirmed dengue at 12 months and 90.4% efficacy against hospitalization at 18 months. Long-term data sustained these results over 4.5 years. Takeda has partnered with Biological E to manufacture up to 50 million doses annually in India, though pricing details are pending.
查看新闻五要素
时间
发布于 2026 年 7 月 20 日 22:03(美国标准时间)。
地点与对象
印度;疫苗 批准用于 4 至 60 岁的个人。
事件
印度药品监管总局 (DCGI) 批准了武田生物制药印度公司的登革热 疫苗、QDENGA,这是印度首个获批的登革热 疫苗。
规模、影响与风险
疫苗 旨在预防所有四种 登革病毒 菌株(DENV-1、DENV-2、DENV-3、DENV-4)。临床试验表明,12 个月时对病毒学确诊的登革热有 80.2% 的疗效,18 个月时对登革热相关住院的疗效为 90.4%。长期数据表明,4.5 年预防住院治疗的有效性为 84.1%。
应对、状态与不确定性
The vaccine is administered as a two-dose subcutaneous regimen spaced three months apart and does not require pre-vaccination testing. Takeda has partnered with Biological E to produce up to 50 million doses annually, though pricing is not yet finalised. The approval is supported by the Phase III TIDES trial and a specific Phase III trial in Indian participants.
B routine priorityPublic-health news

DCGI clears Takeda's QDENGA, India's first-ever dengue vaccine: Full details inside - WION

News Brief
The Drug Controller General of India (DCGI) has approved QDENGA, developed by Takeda Biopharmaceuticals India, as the country's first dengue vaccine. The vaccine is approved for individuals aged four to 60 and offers protection against all four dengue virus serotypes without requiring pre-vaccination testing. It is administered in a two-dose regimen given three months apart. Clinical trials, including a pivotal Phase III study involving over 20,000 participants, showed 80.2% efficacy against virologically confirmed dengue at 12 months and 90.4% efficacy against hospitalization at 18 months. Long-term data sustained these results over 4.5 years. Takeda has partnered with Biological E to manufacture up to 50 million doses annually in India, though pricing details are pending.

Photograph: (Magnific)Story highlightsThe vaccine offers protection against all four strains of the dengue virus and is approved for people aged four to 60, irrespective of whether they've had dengue before, meaning no prior testing is required before vaccination.The Drug Controller General of India (DCGI) has approved Japanese company Takeda Biopharmaceuticals India’s dengue vaccine, QDENGA. It is the first such vaccine that India has greenlighted. The approved vaccine is designed to protect against all four dengue virus strains (DENV-1, DENV-2, DENV-3 and DENV-4) and can be administered despite prior dengue exposure. The company confirmed that the vaccine does not require pre-vaccination testing.The vaccine is given as an under-the-skin (subcutaneous) injection, administered in a two-dose regimen of 0.5 mL each, which is spaced three months apart. While pricing has not been finalised yet, but are expected to be affordable for Indians, given that Takeda has partnered with Hyderabad-based Biological E to produce up to 50 million doses annually. The final price will also hinge on whether the vaccine is eventually included in government-run immunisation schemes.The vaccine offers protection against all four strains of the dengue virus and is approved for people aged four to 60, irrespective of whether they've had dengue before, meaning no prior testing is required before vaccination. Long-term data at 4.5 years showed 84.1 per cent efficacy in preventing hospitalisations, with safety and efficacy sustained for up to seven years across all four dengue serotypes.The approval is further supported by DEN-302, a Phase III trial conducted specifically in Indian participants aged 4–60, which confirmed the vaccine's tolerability, safety, and immunogenicity across children, adolescents, and adults. Ongoing real-world evidence and pharmacovigilance continue to reinforce these findings.WHO also gave a nod for the vaccine, confirming that it meets global standards for quality, safety and efficacy, and allowing eligibility for procurement using international agencies, including UNICEF and PAHO, to support broader access.About the AuthorVinay Prasad SharmaVinay Prasad SharmaVinay Prasad Sharma is a Delhi-based journalist with over three years of newsroom experience, currently working as a Sub-Editor at WION. View five news elements
Time
Published on July 20, 2026, at 22:03 IST.
Location and population
India; the vaccine is approved for individuals aged four to 60 years.
Event
The Drug Controller General of India (DCGI) approved Takeda Biopharmaceuticals India’s dengue vaccine, QDENGA, marking India's first approved dengue vaccine.
Scale, impact and risk
The vaccine is designed to protect against all four dengue virus strains (DENV-1, DENV-2, DENV-3, DENV-4). Clinical trials demonstrated 80.2% efficacy against virologically confirmed dengue at 12 months and 90.4% efficacy against dengue-related hospitalization at 18 months. Long-term data indicated 84.1% efficacy in preventing hospitalizations at 4.5 years.
Response, status and uncertainty
The vaccine is administered as a two-dose subcutaneous regimen spaced three months apart and does not require pre-vaccination testing. Takeda has partnered with Biological E to produce up to 50 million doses annually, though pricing is not yet finalised. The approval is supported by the Phase III TIDES trial and a specific Phase III trial in Indian participants.

斯里兰卡的登革热危机暴露了医疗保健缺口和气候驱动疾病日益严重的威胁

Sri Lanka's Dengue Crisis Exposes Healthcare Gaps and the Rising Threat of Climate-Driven Diseases
新闻简报斯里兰卡正面临近十年来最严重的疫情,自 2026 年 1 月以来已报告感染病例超过 76,000 例,死亡人数超过 53 人。病例激增已使医疗机构不堪重负,迫使当局改建医院病房,并建议症状较轻的患者在家中康复。为了应对危机,政府部署了军队、警察和空军无人机来加强灭蚊工作。暴发 强调了该国对气候敏感疾病的脆弱性,并正在测试其公共卫生系统的恢复能力。官员们还在考虑引入登革热 疫苗,尽管实施面临科学和财务挑战。
查看新闻五要素
时间
发布于 2026 年 7 月 22 日;报告 2026 年 1 月以来发生的感染和死亡情况。
地点与对象
斯里兰卡;特别是影响医疗机构和医院的普通民众。
事件
据媒体报道,斯里兰卡正在经历近十年来最严重的登革热暴发,自1月份以来已确诊超过76,000人感染,53人死亡。
规模、影响与风险
暴发 已导致超过 76,000 例感染报告和 53 例死亡,医院不堪重负,迫使病房转换,并威胁到常规医疗服务的资源。
应对、状态与不确定性
当局部署了军队、警察和空军无人机来消灭蚊子监测,并建议症状较轻的患者在家中康复;政府还在考虑实施登革热 疫苗。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

Sri Lanka's Dengue Crisis Exposes Healthcare Gaps and the Rising Threat of Climate-Driven Diseases

News Brief
Sri Lanka is facing its most severe dengue outbreak in nearly a decade, with over 76,000 reported infections and 53 deaths since January 2026. The surge in cases has overwhelmed healthcare facilities, forcing authorities to convert hospital wards and advise patients with mild symptoms to recover at home. In response to the crisis, the government has deployed the military, police, and air force drones to enhance mosquito surveillance efforts. The outbreak highlights the country's vulnerability to climate-sensitive diseases and is testing the resilience of its public health system. Officials are also considering the introduction of a dengue vaccine, though implementation faces scientific and financial challenges.
Original source text (English)
About Career Advertisement Media Partnership Contact Us HOME NEWS RESEARCH LIVE DISCOURSE PERSPECTIVES AND INSIGHTS SUBMIT PRESS RELEASE NEWS DESK RESEARCH LIVE DISCOURSE PERSPECTIVES AND INSIGHTS INTERVIEW Agro-Forestry Art & Culture Technology Economy Education Energy Politics Law & Governance Health Science Social Sports Transport Urban Development WASH Home News Health Article Sri Lanka's Dengue Crisis Exposes Healthcare Gaps and the Rising Threat of Climate-Driven Diseases Sri Lanka's worst dengue outbreak in nearly a decade is testing the country's healthcare resilience, with military support, drone surveillance, and overwhelmed hospitals highlighting the scale of the crisis. The outbreak underscores the growing need for climate-resilient public health systems, stronger disease surveillance, and long-term vector-control strategies across the region. Devdiscourse News Desk | Colombo | Updated: 22-07-2026 09:51 IST | Created: 22-07-2026 09:51 IST Representative Image. Country: Sri Lanka SHARE Key Takeaways AI Summary Analyzing article... Sri Lanka's most severe dengue outbreak in nearly a decade represents more than a seasonal health emergency. With more than 76,000 reported infections and 53 deaths since January, the crisis is exposing the country's vulnerability to climate-sensitive diseases, testing the resilience of its healthcare system, and demonstrating how infectious disease outbreaks are increasingly becoming governance and national preparedness challenges. The government's decision to deploy the military, police, and air force drones for mosquito surveillance reflects the extraordinary scale of the response required to contain the outbreak. It also signals that traditional public health measures alone may no longer be sufficient as urbanization, extreme weather, and environmental change create more favorable conditions for mosquito-borne diseases. From Health Emergency to National Resilience Test The immediate impact on Sri Lanka extends well beyond rising hospital admissions. Healthcare facilities are already struggling with the influx of dengue patients, forcing authorities to convert hospital wards while advising patients with mild symptoms to recover at home. If transmission continues at the current pace, hospitals may need to divert additional medical staff, beds, and financial resources toward dengue treatment, potentially affecting routine healthcare services such as maternal care, chronic disease management, childhood immunization, and elective procedures. The outbreak also arrives as Sri Lanka continues recovering from recent economic challenges and the effects of Cyclone Ditwah, which placed additional pressure on public infrastructure. Prolonged disease outbreaks increase government healthcare expenditure, reduce workforce productivity, interrupt school attendance, and may discourage tourism if international health concerns intensify. For a country seeking stable economic recovery, maintaining public health has become closely linked to maintaining economic confidence. Climate Change Is Rewriting the Dengue Playbook One of the most significant lessons emerging from the outbreak is the growing connection between climate change and infectious diseases. Heavy rainfall, flooding, and stagnant water following extreme weather events create ideal breeding conditions for Aedes mosquitoes, while rising temperatures can extend mosquito activity throughout the year. Although dengue has long affected Sri Lanka, the increasing frequency and scale of outbreaks suggest that environmental conditions are becoming more favorable for sustained transmission. The deployment of drones to identify water-collecting rooftops demonstrates how technology is beginning to complement traditional mosquito-control efforts. However, surveillance alone cannot solve the problem. Sustainable control will require better urban planning, improved drainage systems, stronger waste management, continuous environmental monitoring, and greater community participation in eliminating mosquito breeding sites. The outbreak therefore reflects a broader global trend in which climate adaptation and public health policy are becoming increasingly interconnected. A Defining Challenge for Policymakers For policymakers, the crisis highlights the need to move beyond reactive outbreak management toward long-term prevention. While emergency measures such as military deployment and intensified inspections may reduce immediate transmission, lasting success depends on strengthening disease surveillance systems, investing in laboratory capacity, improving local government coordination, and expanding public awareness campaigns throughout the year rather than only during outbreaks. Authorities are also considering introducing a dengue vaccine, but vaccine implementation presents scientific, regulatory, and financial challenges because dengue consists of four virus serotypes that complicate immunization strategies. Policymakers will need to balance investment in vaccination with continued funding for mosquito control, sanitation, research, and healthcare infrastructure. The crisis also demonstrates the importance of integrating disaster management, environmental protection, urban development, and public health planning. Rather than treating dengue as an isolated health issue, future policy may increasingly focus on building climate-resilient communities capable of reducing disease risks before outbreaks emerge. Why Stakeholders Have a Shared Responsibility The outbreak carries significant implications for multiple stakeholders across Sri Lankan society. Healthcare professionals face growing pressure to manage rising patient numbers while maintaining essential medical services. Local governments must strengthen sanitation, drainage maintenance, and waste collection to reduce mosquito breeding environments. Educational institutions may need to intensify awareness campaigns as schools remain vulnerable to mosquito transmission during peak seasons. Businesses and employers also have a role in protecting workplaces through mosquito-control measures, while sectors such as tourism, hospitality, and transportation have an interest in maintaining public confidence in Sri Lanka's health management capacity. Communities themselves remain central to the response, since eliminating household breeding sites is one of the most effective methods of reducing mosquito populations. International organizations, development agencies, and regional public health networks may view Sri Lanka's experience as another indication that vector-borne diseases require stronger cross-border cooperation. Support for surveillance technologies, climate-health research, laboratory strengthening, and vaccine development could become increasingly important as dengue spreads across tropical and subtropical regions. Sri Lanka's dengue outbreak is not only a test of emergency response but also of long-term governance. The country's ability to combine healthcare preparedness, environmental management, technological innovation, and community participation will determine whether future outbreaks become more manageable or continue to place growing pressure on public health systems. As climate variability and urban expansion reshape disease patterns across Asia, Sri Lanka's response may offer important lessons for policymakers and stakeholders confronting similar public health challenges. 印度批准首个登革热 疫苗 - 海湾新闻

India approves its first dengue vaccine - Gulf News
新闻简报印度已批准首个治疗登革热的 疫苗,授权武田的 Qdenga 用于 4 至 60 岁的个体。中央药物标准控制组织在审查质量、安全性和有效性数据(包括在印度进行的关键 III 期试验)后授予了营销授权。两剂 疫苗 可预防所有四种 登革病毒 血清型,旨在加强该国对蚊媒疾病的应对。卫生官员强调,疫苗 将补充 媒介 控制和 监测 等现有策略,而不是取代它们。这项批准之际,印度面临着巨大的公共卫生负担,自 2021 年以来已记录了超过 100 万例病例和至少 1,500 人死亡。
查看新闻五要素
时间
该批准由卫生和家庭福利部于2026年7月21日星期二宣布。文章最后更新于2026年7月21日14:19。
地点与对象
该监管批准由印度中央药品标准控制组织(CDSCO)颁发。疫苗 授权用于 4 岁至 60 岁之间的个人。
事件
印度批准了其第一个登革热药物 疫苗,即武田的 Qdenga,用于预防登革热。中央药物标准控制组织授予两剂 疫苗 的营销授权,该药物旨在预防所有四种 登革病毒 血清型。
规模、影响与风险
The approval aims to strengthen the response to dengue, which the World Health Organisation estimates causes 100-400 million infections annually. India has recorded more than one million dengue cases and at least 1,500 deaths since 2021. The vaccine is intended to complement existing control measures.
应对、状态与不确定性
卫生部表示,疫苗接种将加强媒介控制、疾病监测、早期诊断、病例管理等现有措施。此次批准得到了超过八个国家研究的临床数据以及印度一项关键的 III 期试验的支持。完整的原始报告正文已经过审查。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

India approves its first dengue vaccine - Gulf News

News Brief
India has approved its first vaccine for dengue, authorizing Takeda’s Qdenga for use in individuals aged four to 60. The Central Drugs Standard Control Organisation granted marketing authorisation following a review of quality, safety, and efficacy data, including a pivotal Phase III trial in India. The two-dose vaccine protects against all four dengue virus serotypes and is intended to bolster the country's response to the mosquito-borne disease. Health officials emphasized that the vaccine will complement existing strategies like vector control and surveillance rather than replace them. This approval comes as India faces a significant public health burden, having recorded over one million cases and at least 1,500 deaths since 2021.
Original source text (English)
DUBAI 31°CGOLD/FOREXPRAYER TIMESNEWSLETTERSEPAPER GOLD/FOREXDUBAI 31°CPRAYER TIMES ASIAASIAINDIAPAKISTANPHILIPPINES India approves its first dengue vaccineWorld / Asia / IndiaIndia approves its first dengue vaccineTakeda’s two-dose Qdenga jab cleared for people aged 4-60 amid rising infectionsLast updated: July 21, 2026 | 14:19Alex Abraham, Senior Associate Editor 3 MIN READAdd as a preferred source on GoogleIndia has approved its first vaccine to prevent dengue, clearing Qdenga to be used in people aged between four and 60 years.BloombergNew Delhi: India has approved its first vaccine to prevent dengue, clearing Japanese pharmaceutical company Takeda’s Qdenga for use in people aged between four and 60 years as the country seeks to strengthen its response to one of the world’s fastest-growing mosquito-borne diseases.The Central Drugs Standard Control Organisation (CDSCO), India’s drug regulator, granted marketing authorisation for the vaccine after reviewing its quality, safety and efficacy, the Ministry of Health and Family Welfare said on Tuesday.The approval allows Takeda Biopharmaceuticals India Pvt. Ltd. to import and market the vaccine in the country.Qdenga is designed to protect against all four known dengue virus serotypes and is administered as two 0.5 ml injections given three months apart. The vaccine is produced using recombinant DNA technology and is supplied as a freeze-dried powder for reconstitution before being administered as a subcutaneous injection.Also Read: Chennai steps up dengue prevention measures as cases riseThe health ministry said the approval was supported by extensive clinical data from studies conducted in more than eight countries, including Brazil, Thailand and Sri Lanka, as well as a pivotal Phase III clinical trial carried out in India involving participants aged four to 60 years.SPONSORED LINKS BY PROJECT AGORACognitive Decline Begins When Seniors Say These 3 Phrases. “登革热季节”不再存在:对于印度人来说,断骨热成为全年的祸害 - Gavi、疫苗 联盟

No more “dengue season”: for Indians, breakbone fever becomes year-round scourge - Gavi, the Vaccine Alliance
新闻简报Gavi、疫苗 联盟报告称,登革热 在印度不再是季节性威胁,而是已演变成全年的公共卫生挑战。据报道,德里的居民在季风季节到来之前就已经在与这种病毒作斗争,传统上,季风季节与登革热 传播 有关。为了应对这种不断变化的流行病学形势,报告指出,印度已宣布首次批准登革热 疫苗。这一进展标志着该国在控制这种疾病负担方面迈出了重要一步,这种疾病通常被称为断骨热,这种疾病全年持续影响着人口。
查看新闻五要素
时间
发布于 2026 年 7 月 21 日。
地点与对象
印度,特别提到德里的居民。
事件
Gavi、疫苗 联盟报告称,登革热 已成为印度全年的祸害,德里居民在季风季节之前与该病毒作斗争。该报告还指出,印度宣布首次批准登革热 疫苗。
规模、影响与风险
文章将登革热描述为“全年祸害”和“断骨热”,表明登革热对健康产生重大且持续的影响,尽管所提供的证据中没有报告具体病例数或死亡人数。
应对、状态与不确定性
印度宣布首次批准登革热 疫苗 作为应对措施。该报告强调了 传播 模式与特定季节的转变。完整的原始文章正文已经过审核。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

No more “dengue season”: for Indians, breakbone fever becomes year-round scourge - Gavi, the Vaccine Alliance

News Brief
Gavi, the Vaccine Alliance reports that dengue fever is no longer a seasonal threat in India but has evolved into a year-round public health challenge. Residents in Delhi are now reported to be contending with the virus well before the arrival of the monsoon season, traditionally associated with dengue transmission. In response to this shifting epidemiological landscape, the report notes that India has announced its first-ever approval of a dengue vaccine. This development marks a significant step in the country's efforts to manage the burden of the disease, often referred to as breakbone fever, which continues to impact the population throughout the year.

B routine priorityPublic-health news

Do People With Type 2 Diabetes Face A Higher Risk of Severe Dengue? New Review Says Yes - Health and Me

News Brief
A recent review published by Health and Me on July 21, 2026, suggests a potential link between type 2 diabetes and an increased risk of severe dengue. The review, which synthesizes existing evidence, indicates that individuals with type 2 diabetes may experience more severe outcomes if infected with the dengue virus. However, the report does not provide specific data on case numbers, mortality rates, or geographical locations. The findings highlight a possible vulnerability in diabetic populations during dengue outbreaks, though further research is needed to establish a definitive causal relationship and quantify the risk. The review serves as an important reminder for public health officials to consider comorbidities in dengue prevention and management strategies.

B 常规重要公共卫生新闻

抗病毒药物 在登革热预防中的潜力和 治疗 - Contagion Live

The Potential for Antivirals in Dengue Prophylaxis and Treatment - Contagion Live
新闻简报最近的一项 2a 期试验使用 DENV-3 受控人类感染模型评估了研究中的 抗病毒药物 mosnodenvir 预防登革热的作用。该研究在约翰·霍普金斯大学和佛蒙特大学进行,招募了以前没有接触过登革热的健康成年人。结果表明,与安慰剂相比,高剂量莫诺登韦显着降低了 DENV-3 RNA 水平和感染相关症状,表现出剂量依赖性 抗病毒药物 效应。该药物总体耐受性良好,不良事件大多轻微。尽管取得了这些有希望的结果,但研究人员指出,该研究的局限性,包括样本量小和受控设置,限制了对现实世界 暴发 的推广。值得注意的是,强生公司已停止该药物的开发,而其他登革热 抗病毒药物 候选药物仍在继续接受临床评估。
查看新闻五要素
时间
公布日期为 2026 年 7 月 22 日。所引用的 2a 期试验对参与者进行了从第 0 天到第 85 天的跟踪。
地点与对象
该研究是在约翰霍普金斯大学和佛蒙特大学进行的。该研究涉及 18 至 55 岁的健康成年人,之前没有接触过登革热病毒,也没有登革热流行病史。
事件
一项 2a 期、双盲、随机、安慰剂对照试验使用 DENV-3 对照人类感染模型评估了 mosnodenvir 作为 登革病毒 感染预防药物的 抗病毒药物 活性和安全性。
规模、影响与风险
与安慰剂相比,高剂量莫诺登韦可显着降低 DENV-3 RNA 水平和登革热感染相关症状。该药物通常耐受性良好,不良事件大多为轻度至中度。
应对、状态与不确定性
研究结果支持莫诺登韦作为一种有前途的 抗病毒药物 用于登革热预防,但普遍性受到样本量小、使用低减毒 DENV-3 菌株和受控暴露的限制。强生公司已停止进一步开发该药物,但其他 抗病毒药物 策略仍在开发中。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

The Potential for Antivirals in Dengue Prophylaxis and Treatment - Contagion Live

News Brief
A recent phase 2a trial evaluated the investigational antiviral mosnodenvir for dengue prophylaxis using a controlled human infection model with DENV-3. Conducted at Johns Hopkins University and the University of Vermont, the study enrolled healthy adults with no prior dengue exposure. Results indicated that high-dose mosnodenvir significantly reduced DENV-3 RNA levels and infection-related symptoms compared to placebo, demonstrating a dose-dependent antiviral effect. The drug was generally well tolerated, with mostly mild adverse events. Despite these promising results, researchers note that the study's limitations, including the small sample size and controlled setting, restrict generalizability to real-world outbreaks. Notably, Johnson & Johnson has discontinued the drug's development, while other dengue antiviral candidates continue to undergo clinical evaluation.
Original source text (English)
A study found that the investigational antiviral mosnodenvir reduced dengue virus infection RNA levels and symptoms in a controlled setting, supporting its potential as a prophylactic option. Dengue virus infection, transmitted by the Aedes mosquito and caused by 4 distinct serotypes (DENV- 1-4), has an expanding global incidence and geographic range 1 with locally acquired cases to Europe and the United States. 2 While its cases are often mild or asymptomatic, dengue has the potential to cause severe illness, including shock and capillary leak syndrome, in both dengue-naive individuals and those with prior infection. Two vaccines have received worldwide approval for the prevention and long-term protection against reinfection. CYD-TDV (Dengvaxia; Sanofi) is approved for use in certain endemic areas among those aged 9 to 45 years with confirmed prior dengue infection. However, it is not approved for travelers and is being discontinued due to low demand. TAK-003 (Qdenga; Takeda) is approved in many countries for both seropositive and seronegative people. Despite vaccine development and availability, there are no approved antiviral prophylaxis or treatments against dengue, especially in those who are nonimmune. Mosnodenvir is an orally administered small-molecule agent developed to target dengue viral replication. The drug inhibits the de novo formation of the NS4B-NS3 complex. NS4B is a transmembrane protein in the endoplasmic reticulum, which binds NS3 to enhance NS3 helicase activity and increase viral replication. 3 Preclinical studies have shown serotype-independent antiviral activity through in vitro and in vivo models, and recent phase 1 trials have demonstrated an acceptable safety profile. 4 This FIGURE represents the viral replication complex of Dengue virus and the target of mosnodenvir. The NS4B complex is ER (endoplasmic reticulum) bound. 5 Durbin and colleagues conducted a phase 2a trial that assessed the antiviral activity and safety of mosnodenvir in healthy participants who received a challenge virus with a hypoattenuated strain of DENV-3. 6 This was designed as a doubleblind, randomized, placebo-controlled trial at Johns Hopkins University and the University of Vermont, with funding from Johnson & Johnson. Healthy adults aged 18 to 55 years with no prior dengue exposure or endemic travel were enrolled and assigned to mosnodenvir or placebo in a stepwise, dose-escalation design. Participants were randomly assigned 1:1 to receive a 200-mg loading dose of oral mosnodenvir or placebo for the 5 days prior to challenge, and received an inoculation of underattenuated DENV-3 on day 0. The participants were then enrolled 2:1 into a high-dose cohort to establish safety. Further participants were randomly assigned to a high (100 mg), medium (50 mg), or low (10 mg) dose vs placebo in a 3:3:1 ratio for maintenance from day 1 to day 21. Patients were then followed through day 85. Primary outcomes included a reduction in DENV-3 RNA levels, clinical symptoms of infection, serologic response, and safety. The trial was powered to 85% probability to detect a 30% difference in RNA load between groups. High-dose mosnodenvir was associated with a significant reduction in DENV-3 RNA levels compared with placebo, with a clear dose-dependent antiviral effect regarding reduction in RNA levels and dengue infection–related symptoms. Participants with undetectable viral RNA did not have any detectable neutralizing antibodies, suggesting that successful inhibition of viral replication may prevent the development of humoral immunity. Mosnodenvir was generally well tolerated, with mostly mild to moderate adverse events. Only 2 severe events were noted: infection with SARS-CoV-2, and severe elevation in lipase and glucose levels. Together, the findings support mosnodenvir as a promising antiviral for dengue prophylaxis against DENV-3 infection, especially at high doses. However, the small sample size, use of a hypoattenuated DENV-3 challenge strain, and controlled timing of exposure limit generalizability to real-world settings with unpredictable exposure and multiple circulating serotypes. There is further work to be done to study those who have already been infected, those living in endemic regions, and the timing of prophylaxis. Despite these encouraging early results, Johnson & Johnson has discontinued further development of mosnodenvir. 7 However, other dengue antiviral strategies remain in development, including agents that target antiviral replication by Novartis, which are undergoing a phase 2 trial. 8 There is also a monoclonal antibody undergoing evaluation. 9 These show promise for the future of prophylaxis and treatment for dengue virus in the seropositive and seronegative populations. Paper: Durbin AP, Van Wesenbeeck L, Pierce KK, et al. Daily mosnodenvir as dengue prophylaxis in a controlled human infection model. View five news elements
Time
Publication date reported as July 22, 2026. The cited phase 2a trial followed participants from day 0 to day 85.
Location and population
The study was conducted at Johns Hopkins University and the University of Vermont. It involved healthy adults aged 18 to 55 years with no prior dengue exposure or endemic travel.
Event
A phase 2a, double-blind, randomized, placebo-controlled trial assessed the antiviral activity and safety of mosnodenvir as a prophylactic against dengue virus infection using a controlled human infection model with DENV-3.
Scale, impact and risk
High-dose mosnodenvir was associated with a significant reduction in DENV-3 RNA levels and dengue infection-related symptoms compared to placebo. The drug was generally well tolerated with mostly mild to moderate adverse events.
Response, status and uncertainty
Findings support mosnodenvir as a promising antiviral for dengue prophylaxis, but generalizability is limited by the small sample size, use of a hypoattenuated DENV-3 strain, and controlled exposure. Johnson & Johnson has discontinued further development of the drug, though other antiviral strategies remain in development.

斯里兰卡正在与近十年来最严重的登革热作斗争 - 《独立报》

Sri Lanka battles its worst dengue outbreak in almost a decade - The Independent
新闻简报斯里兰卡正面临近十年来最严重的 重症登革热 暴发,卫生官员报告称,自 2026 年 1 月以来,已有超过 77,000 例感染病例和 56 例死亡病例。病例激增归因于强效 DENV-2 毒株和飓风迪特瓦 (Ditwah) 产生的滋生地。医院,尤其是科伦坡的医院,承受着巨大的压力,需要增加床位并延长工作时间来应对病人的涌入。作为回应,政府部署了军用无人机来探测蚊子滋生地点,建立了一个监控单位,并授权警方检查家庭和公共区域。虽然当局正在考虑登革热 疫苗,但公共卫生专家警告称,由于该病毒的复杂性和多种流行毒株,其有效性有限。

2026 年 7 月 21 日报道;自 2026 年 1 月以来报告的感染和死亡人数。
地点与对象
斯里兰卡,特别提到科伦坡等高风险地区。
事件
斯里兰卡正在经历近十年来最严重的登革热 暴发,这归因于 DENV-2 毒株和飓风迪特瓦的影响。
规模、影响与风险
自 1 月份以来,报告了超过 77,000 例感染和 56 例死亡;由于患者大量涌入,医院承受着巨大的压力。
应对、状态与不确定性
政府部署了军用无人机来识别繁殖地点,建立了监控小组,警察正在检查房屋。当局正在考虑登革热 疫苗,但专家警告其益处有限。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

Sri Lanka battles its worst dengue outbreak in almost a decade - The Independent

News Brief
Sri Lanka is facing its most severe dengue outbreak in nearly a decade, with health officials reporting over 77,000 infections and 56 deaths since January 2026. The surge is attributed to the virulent DENV-2 strain and breeding grounds created by Cyclone Ditwah. Hospitals, particularly in Colombo, are under significant strain, adding beds and extending staff hours to manage the patient influx. In response, the government has deployed military drones to detect mosquito breeding sites, established a monitoring unit, and authorized police inspections of homes and public areas. While authorities are considering a dengue vaccine, public health experts have cautioned about its limited effectiveness due to the virus's complexity and multiple circulating strains.
Original source text (English)

Qdenga 登革热 疫苗 是什么?有效性、安全性和可用性

What Is Qdenga Dengue Vaccine? Effectiveness, Safety and Availability
新闻简报印度已批准 Qdenga (TAK-003),这是武田开发的第一个用于预防登革热的 疫苗。印度药品监管总局授予两剂四价减毒活疫苗 疫苗 的上市许可,适用于 4 至 60 岁的个人。与之前的 疫苗 不同,Qdenga 不需要在 疫苗接种 之前筛查之前的登革热感染情况。临床试验表明,对于与登革热相关的住院治疗有大约 84% 的保护,对于病毒学确诊的登革热则有 61% 的保护。疫苗 可预防所有四种血清型,并已获得 WHO 资格预审。虽然商业发布日期和定价尚未确定,但 疫苗 预计将首先通过私人医疗保健市场上市,并将补充现有的 媒介 控制措施。

时间
发表于2026-07-22;尽管所提供的证据中没有报告具体的批准日期,但批准是在全面的科学评估之后进行的。

印度;疫苗 批准用于 4 至 60 岁的个人。

印度药品监管总局 (DCGI) 授予 Qdenga (TAK-003) 营销授权,这是印度首个用于预防登革热的 疫苗,由武田 (Takeda) 开发。
规模、影响与风险
疫苗 是一种四价减毒活 疫苗,可预防所有四种登革热血清型。临床试验表明,4.5 年后,约 84% 的药物可预防登革热相关住院治疗,61% 的药物可预防病毒学确诊的登革热。监测在其他国家上市后已显示出良好的安全性。
应对、状态与不确定性
疫苗 将补充现有的蚊虫控制措施。商业发布日期、价格以及是否纳入政府 疫苗接种 计划尚不清楚。有关处方条件的更多详细信息预计将在发布后公布。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

What Is Qdenga Dengue Vaccine? Effectiveness, Safety and Availability

News Brief
India has approved Qdenga (TAK-003), its first vaccine for the prevention of dengue, developed by Takeda. The Drug Controller General of India granted marketing authorisation for the two-dose, live-attenuated tetravalent vaccine for individuals aged four to 60 years. Unlike previous vaccines, Qdenga does not require pre-vaccination screening for prior dengue infection. Clinical trials demonstrated approximately 84% protection against dengue-related hospitalisation and 61% against virologically confirmed dengue. The vaccine protects against all four serotypes and has received WHO prequalification. While the commercial launch date and pricing are pending, the vaccine is expected to be available initially through the private healthcare market and will complement existing vector control measures.
Original source text (English)
Qdenga has been approved in India for individuals aged four to 60 years. India has approved Qdenga, its first vaccine for preventing dengue. The two-dose vaccine is approved for people aged four to 60 years. It protects against all four dengue serotypes without prior infection screening. India has approved its first vaccine for the prevention of dengue, adding a new tool to the country’s efforts to tackle the mosquito-borne viral disease. The Drug Controller General of India (DCGI) , has granted marketing authorisation to Qdenga, a tetravalent dengue vaccine developed by Japanese pharmaceutical company Takeda. The vaccine is manufactured by Takeda GmbH in Germany and will be imported by Takeda Biopharmaceuticals India. The approval covers people aged between four and 60 years and follows an assessment of the vaccine’s quality, safety and effectiveness under the Drugs and Cosmetics Act, 1940, and the Drugs Rules, 1945. Qdenga, also known as TAK-003, is a live-attenuated tetravalent vaccine. This means it uses weakened forms of the dengue virus to help the immune system develop protection against all four dengue virus serotypes: DENV-1, DENV-2, DENV-3 and DENV-4. The vaccine has been developed using recombinant DNA technology. According to the Health Ministry, genes that encode serotype-specific surface proteins are engineered into a dengue virus type-2 backbone. Qdenga is supplied as a freeze-dried powder that must be reconstituted before use. It is administered through a subcutaneous injection, which means the dose is given under the skin. Winning The Golden Hour: How Haryana Showcases A Better Way To Fight Non-Communicable Diseases – Dr. Sumita Misra India Approves First Dengue Vaccine For People Aged 4 To 60 Gig Economy Taking Toll On Young Delivery Riders' Bodies And Minds: ICMR Study Cyclospora Outbreak In US: What Is Food-Borne Parasite And Does It Pose Risk In India? The approved vaccination schedule consists of two doses of 0.5 ml each, administered three months apart. Qdenga has been approved in India for individuals aged four to 60 years. A key feature of the vaccine is that it can be administered regardless of whether the recipient has previously been infected with dengue . Unlike Dengvaxia, an older dengue vaccine that is generally recommended only for people with evidence of a previous dengue infection, Qdenga does not require pre-vaccination blood screening. This could make its use comparatively simpler, particularly in large-scale vaccination programmes, as eligible recipients would not need to undergo a test to confirm a past dengue infection before receiving the vaccine. Further details on prescribing conditions, precautions and the groups prioritised for vaccination are expected to become clearer when the vaccine is commercially launched in India. Evidence supporting the vaccine includes the TIDES Phase III clinical trial, which involved more than 20,000 children and adolescents across eight dengue-endemic countries. After about four-and-a-half years of follow-up, the vaccine showed around 84 per cent protection against dengue-related hospitalisation and approximately 61 per cent protection against virologically confirmed dengue. The findings also indicated continued protection against severe disease among people with and without a previous dengue infection. However, the level of effectiveness varied across the four dengue serotypes. The Indian regulatory review also included a Phase III safety and immunogenicity study covering participants aged four to 60 years in India. Qdenga has received World Health Organization prequalification, indicating that it meets international requirements related to quality, safety and efficacy. This also allows global agencies such as UNICEF and the Pan American Health Organization to procure the vaccine. According to the Health Ministry, the approval followed a “comprehensive scientific evaluation” of the vaccine’s quality, safety and efficacy. Qdenga has already been approved in 42 countries, including the United Kingdom, European Union nations, Switzerland, Indonesia, Malaysia and Thailand. More than 24 million doses have been distributed globally. The government said post-marketing surveillance in countries where the vaccine is already being used has shown a favourable safety profile, with no significant safety concerns identified. As Qdenga is a live-attenuated vaccine, recipients should consult a doctor regarding eligibility, particularly if they have medical conditions, weakened immunity or are taking medicines that affect the immune system. Takeda has not yet announced the vaccine’s commercial launch date or price in India. Initially, it could be made available through the private healthcare market, although details of its rollout remain awaited. Takeda has also entered into a partnership with Hyderabad-based Biological E to manufacture up to 50 million doses annually. Local production could improve supply and potentially make the vaccine more affordable over time. It is not yet clear whether Qdenga will eventually be included in a government vaccination programme. The vaccine will complement, rather than replace, existing measures such as mosquito control, eliminating breeding sites, early diagnosis, disease surveillance and timely treatment. Its approval nevertheless represents a significant shift in India’s dengue strategy, particularly as seasonal outbreaks continue to place pressure on hospitals during the monsoon and post-monsoon periods. View five news elements
Time
Published on 2026-07-22; the approval follows a comprehensive scientific evaluation, though the specific event date of approval was not reported in the supplied evidence.
Location and population
India; the vaccine is approved for individuals aged four to 60 years.
Event
The Drug Controller General of India (DCGI) granted marketing authorisation to Qdenga (TAK-003), India's first vaccine for preventing dengue, developed by Takeda.
Scale, impact and risk
The vaccine is a tetravalent live-attenuated vaccine protecting against all four dengue serotypes. Clinical trials indicated approximately 84% protection against dengue-related hospitalisation and 61% against virologically confirmed dengue after 4.5 years. Post-marketing surveillance in other countries has shown a favourable safety profile.
Response, status and uncertainty
The vaccine will complement existing mosquito control measures. Commercial launch date, price, and inclusion in government vaccination programmes are not yet clear. Further details on prescribing conditions are expected upon launch.
