India approves its first dengue vaccine - Manorama Yearbook

News Brief
India has approved its first dengue vaccine, Qdenga, according to the Manorama Yearbook. This development marks a significant step in the country's efforts to combat dengue, a mosquito-borne viral disease that affects millions annually. The vaccine, cleared by the Central Drugs Standard Control Organization (CDSCO), is expected to contribute to public health strategies aimed at reducing the incidence and severity of dengue infections. While the specific details about the vaccine's efficacy, distribution, or impact on disease prevalence are not provided in the evidence, the approval itself is a confirmed event. The source, Manorama Yearbook, is a reputable media outlet, and the information is presented as an official report. This decision reflects India's growing focus on addressing vector-borne diseases through innovative medical solutions.

登革热 和心脏健康:专家警告注意隐藏的心脏风险 - 印度快报

Dengue fever and heart health: Expert warns of hidden cardiac risks to note - The Indian Express
新闻简报随着印度季风季节登革热病例增加,医学专家强调了与感染相关的经常被忽视的心脏风险。介入心脏病学顾问 Chirag D 博士警告说,虽然心脏并发症并不常见,但其范围可以从小问题到危及生命的病症,如心肌炎和急性心力衰竭。由于广泛的血浆渗漏和炎症,重症登革热、登革出血热或登革休克综合征患者的这些风险明显更高。长时间胸痛、呼吸困难和不明原因低血压等症状应立即进行心脏评估。尽管没有针对登革热相关心肌炎的具体 抗病毒药物 诊断,但早期 诊断 和支持护理(包括水合和心脏监测)通常会给轻度心脏受累患者带来良好的康复结果。
查看新闻五要素
时间
发布于 2026 年 7 月 18 日上午 06:00(美国标准时间)。该文章将一般季风季节作为登革热 发病率 增加的背景。
地点与对象
印度,特别提到班加罗尔和阿斯特怀特菲尔德医院。讨论涉及普通人群和患有 登革热 的患者。
事件
医学专家 Chirag D 博士警告说,登革热 可能会导致隐藏的心脏风险,包括心肌炎和心律失常,并强调需要认识并及早 诊断 这些并发症。
规模、影响与风险
心脏表现被描述为并不常见,但可能危及生命,特别是在 重症登革热、登革出血热或登革休克综合征的病例中。风险包括急性心力衰竭和心源性休克。
应对、状态与不确定性
治疗包括补水、心脏监测和重症病例的重症监护。目前尚无针对登革热相关心肌炎的特效 抗病毒药物 药物。本文基于专家意见和公共领域信息。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

Dengue fever and heart health: Expert warns of hidden cardiac risks to note - The Indian Express

News Brief
As dengue cases rise during the monsoon season in India, medical experts are highlighting the often-overlooked cardiac risks associated with the infection. Dr. Chirag D, a consultant in interventional cardiology, warns that while cardiac complications are not very common, they can range from minor issues to life-threatening conditions like myocarditis and acute heart failure. These risks are significantly higher in patients with severe dengue, dengue hemorrhagic fever, or dengue shock syndrome due to extensive plasma leakage and inflammation. Symptoms such as prolonged chest pain, breathing difficulties, and unexplained hypotension should prompt immediate cardiac evaluation. Although no specific antiviral treatment exists for dengue-related myocarditis, early diagnosis and supportive care, including hydration and heart monitoring, generally lead to good recovery outcomes for patients with mild cardiac involvement.
Original source text (English)
Dengue fever and heart health: Expert warns of hidden cardiac risks to note The good news is that many of the patients with mild involvement of the heart recover well if the disease is diagnosed and treated early. By: Lifestyle Desk 4 min read New Delhi Jul 18, 2026 06:00 AM IST Dengue can also impact your heart. (Source: Magnific) With the monsoon comes mosquitoes, and mosquitoes bring with them the fear of dengue fever. Dengue is typically associated with high fever, generalised body aches, and a significant decrease in platelet count. But what many miss out on is the fact that the disease can also cause heart problems. With the increasing incidence of dengue in India, it has become even more crucial for doctors and people alike to understand that it is a multisystemic disease. Dr Chirag D, Consultant – Interventional Cardiology, Aster Whitefield Hospitals, Bengaluru , says that although it is not very common, cardiac manifestations of dengue have been well recognised and can range from minor, reversible conditions to life-threatening diseases in severe dengue infection. DISCLAIMER: This article is based on information from the public domain and/or the experts we spoke to. Always consult your health practitioner before starting any routine. Thus, Dr Chirag says it is crucial to take special note of patients with dengue infection who have symptoms such as prolonged chest pain, difficulty breathing, palpitations, lightheadedness, syncope, or hypotension that are unresponsive to adequate fluid replacement. Dengue virus can mess with the heart in a couple of ways. “It may directly get into the heart muscle, or it can set off an outsized immune reaction that ends up causing inflammation in the heart muscle, basically myocarditis. That swelling can make the heart pump less effectively for a while, and it can also throw off the heart’s electrical pathway, so people may develop unusual heart rhythms,” he tells indianexpress.com. Not all instances of breathlessness and hypotension observed in patients suffering from dengue fever can be explained by dehydration and plasma leakage alone. (Source: Magnific) Quoting studies, Dr Chirag points out that ECG abnormalities such as sinus bradycardia, sinus tachycardia, first-degree atrioventricular block, and non-specific ST-T changes are some of the more commonly seen cardiac findings in dengue patients. Story continues below. Subscribe to see fewer ads. Who’s at risk? The doctor shares that danger for cardiac complications is considerably greater in people with severe dengue , dengue haemorrhagic fever, or dengue shock syndrome. “This happens because there’s extensive plasma leakage along with widespread inflammation, and that whole circulatory instability really adds a lot to the load on the heart,” Dr Chirag explains, further adding that sometimes myocarditis can move on to acute heart failure, cardiogenic shock, or even serious cardiac arrhythmias, so intensive care management becomes necessary. What to note Dr Chirag says that not all instances of breathlessness and hypotension observed in patients suffering from dengue fever can be explained by dehydration and plasma leakage alone. It is essential to evaluate cardiac involvement when there is no significant improvement observed with conventional treatment measures. ALSO READ | Dengue in children: Some early signs parents must pay attention to The good news is that many of the patients with mild involvement of the heart recover well if the disease is diagnosed and treated early. “Currently, there are no specific anti-viral drugs available for the management of myocarditis due to dengue. However, management of the disease includes keeping the patient hydrated and monitoring the functioning of the heart, along with the management of any other complications arising due to the disease,” Dr Chirag concludes. Story continues below. Subscribe to see fewer ads. DISCLAIMER: This article is based on information from the public domain and/or the experts we spoke to. Published on July 18, 2026, at 06:00 AM IST. The article references the general monsoon season as the context for increased dengue incidence.
Location and population
India, with specific mention of Bengaluru and Aster Whitefield Hospitals. The discussion concerns the general population and patients suffering from dengue fever.
Event
A medical expert, Dr. Chirag D, warns that dengue fever can cause hidden cardiac risks, including myocarditis and arrhythmias, and emphasizes the need for awareness and early diagnosis of these complications.
Scale, impact and risk
Cardiac manifestations are described as not very common but potentially life-threatening, particularly in cases of severe dengue, dengue hemorrhagic fever, or dengue shock syndrome. Risks include acute heart failure and cardiogenic shock.
Response, status and uncertainty
Management involves hydration, heart monitoring, and intensive care for severe cases. There are currently no specific antiviral drugs available for dengue-related myocarditis. The article is based on expert opinion and public domain information.

India has approved its first dengue vaccine, with the DCGI granting clearance to Takeda's QDENGA. Designed to protect against all 4 dengue virus strains, the vaccine is approved for people aged 4 to 60 years and does not require prior dengue infection testin - LinkedIn
新闻简报印度药品监管机构 DCGI 已批准武田的 QDENGA,这标志着该国首个获得许可的登革热药物 疫苗。鉴于印度承担着世界近三分之一的登革热负担,且过去二十年登革热病例激增,此次批准意义重大。疫苗 旨在预防所有四种 登革病毒 菌株,被批准用于 4 至 60 岁的个人,并且不需要在 疫苗接种 之前进行感染筛查。临床试验表明,对确诊登革热的疗效超过 80%,对住院治疗的疗效超过 90%。尽管取得了这一里程碑,但成本和纳入公共免疫计划的情况尚未确定,而专家强调,蚊子控制仍然是一项关键的防御措施。

该批准报告于2026年7月22日公布。
地点与对象
该批准由印度药品监管总局 (DCGI) 批准,适用于印度人口,特别针对 4 至 60 岁的个人。
事件
印度批准了其第一个登革热药物 疫苗,即武田的 QDENGA,批准用于对抗所有四种 登革病毒 菌株。
规模、影响与风险
埃克塔·巴特拉解释道。
应对、状态与不确定性
疫苗 不需要事先进行登革热感染检测。虽然这是一个重要的里程碑,但关于处方率、成本和纳入公共免疫计划的可能性仍然存在问题。控制蚊子仍然是主要防御措施。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

India has approved its first dengue vaccine, with the DCGI granting clearance to Takeda's QDENGA. Designed to protect against all 4 dengue virus strains, the vaccine is approved for people aged 4 to 60 years and does not require prior dengue infection testin - LinkedIn

News Brief
India's drug regulator, the DCGI, has approved Takeda's QDENGA, marking the country's first licensed dengue vaccine. The approval is significant given that India bears nearly one-third of the world's dengue burden, with cases surging over the last two decades. Designed to protect against all four dengue virus strains, the vaccine is approved for individuals aged 4 to 60 and does not require pre-vaccination screening for prior infection. Clinical trials indicated over 80% efficacy against confirmed dengue and over 90% efficacy against hospitalization. Despite this milestone, the cost and integration into public immunization programs are yet to be determined, while experts emphasize that mosquito control remains a critical defense measure.
Original source text (English)

Takeda's Queue Denga has become the first dengue vaccine to receive approval from India's drug regulator, the DCGI. Now, why exactly does this matter? Because India accounts for nearly one third of the world's dengue burden, and reported dengue cases have increased 10 to 11 times over the past two decades. Like we know every monsoon, hospitals prepare for a search in cases. So what makes this vaccine? Your friend Hugh Denga is designed to protect against all four dengue virus strains. It's a proof for people aged anywhere between 4 to 60 years and also it does not require blood test to check if you've had dengue before getting vaccinated. Takeda says the vaccine has been studied in more than 28,000 participants globally. In its phase three trial, it showed over 80% efficacy against confirmed dengue after 2 doses and over 90% efficacy against dengue related. Hospitalization at 18 months. The vaccine is already approved in around 43 countries and has been distributed more than 32,000,000 doses worldwide and is recommended by The Who for use in dengue endemic settings. The big question now is how much will it be prescribed in India, what will it cost and whether it will eventually be included in the public immunization programs. While this approval is a major milestone, Mosquito control. Eliminating stagnant water and preventing mosquito bites remains the first line of defense against dengue, according to experts

B 常规重要公共卫生新闻

QDENGA:印度首个获批的登革热 疫苗

QDENGA: India’s first approved dengue vaccine
新闻简报印度已批准其首个登革热 疫苗(QDENGA),由武田生物制药公司(Takeda Biopharmaceuticals)开发。印度药品监管局局长授予了该两剂量方案的市场授权,该方案被批准用于 4 至 60 岁的个人。疫苗 旨在预防 登革病毒 的所有四种血清型。对于承担着全球约三分之一登革热负担的印度来说,这一批准是一项重大的公共卫生进展。虽然 疫苗 的上市标志着预防工作向前迈进了一步,但制造商尚未公布印度市场的官方价格。
查看新闻五要素
时间
发布于 2026 年 7 月 21 日下午 03:08(美国标准时间);更新于 2026 年 7 月 21 日下午 03:10(美国标准时间)。
地点与对象
印度;批准用于 4 至 60 岁的个人。
事件
印度药品监管总局 (DCGI) 向武田生物制药公司生产的登革热 疫苗 QDENGA 授予市场授权,这是首个获准在印度使用的登革热 疫苗。
规模、影响与风险
India accounts for approximately one-third of the global dengue burden; the World Health Organization estimates 100–400 million infections occur globally each year.
应对、状态与不确定性
疫苗 分两剂给药,间隔三个月,旨在预防所有四种登革热血清型;印度市场的官方价格尚未公布。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

QDENGA: India’s first approved dengue vaccine

News Brief
India has approved its first dengue vaccine, QDENGA, developed by Takeda Biopharmaceuticals. The Drug Controller General of India granted market authorisation for the two-dose regimen, which is approved for individuals aged 4 to 60. The vaccine is designed to protect against all four serotypes of the dengue virus. This approval is a significant public health development for India, which bears about one-third of the global dengue burden. While the vaccine's availability marks a step forward in prevention efforts, the manufacturer has not yet announced the official price for the Indian market.
Original source text (English)

India World Sport FIFA 2026 Data Health Opinion Science Entertainment Premium Videos Podcast Photos Visual Stories Specials TH Explains eBooks TH Games Newsletter Lit For Life The Huddle Home Sci-Tech Health QDENGA: India’s first approved dengue vaccine QDENGA, India’s first dengue vaccine, receives approval for use, marking a significant milestone in dengue prevention efforts.Updated - July 21, 2026 03:10 pm IST Zubeda Hamid0 / 0 NewREAD LATER SEE ALL Remove QDENGA, a vaccine for dengue manufactured by Takeda Biopharmaceuticals, a multinational pharmaceutical company headquartered in Japan, has received market authorisation from the Drug Controller General of India (DCGI). This makes it the first dengue vaccine to be approved for use in the country. The vaccine can be used in individuals aged 4 to 60, and is given in two doses, three months apart. It is designed to protect against all four dengue serotypes (variations of the virus). The company has still not announced an official price of the vaccine for India.About half of the world’s population is now at risk of dengue, with an estimated 100–400 million infections occurring each year, according to the World Health Organization. India, which accounts for about one-third of the global burden, is one of five nations that are are among the 30 most highly endemic countries in the world when it comes to dengue. 印度首例登革热疫苗来了;它不能防止蚊虫叮咬 - Onmanorama

India's first dengue vaccine is here; it does not prevent mosquito bites - Onmanorama
新闻简报印度已批准首个登革热 疫苗,该药物旨在减轻登革热感染的严重程度,而不是预防蚊虫叮咬。Onmanorama 于 2026 年 7 月 21 日发表的这篇新闻文章重点介绍了 疫苗 的推出及其对喀拉拉邦公共卫生的潜在影响。该报告没有提供具体的病例数、死亡人数或暴露人数,也没有提及任何明确的风险级别。疫苗 的批准是官方回应,但没有关于其当前实施状态或与登革热相关的正在进行的调查的信息。本文重点介绍了 疫苗 的发展及其在管理登革热方面的作用,但没有详细说明更广泛的公共卫生影响或其在该地区的使用程度。

B routine priorityPublic-health news

India's first dengue vaccine is here; it does not prevent mosquito bites - Onmanorama

News Brief
India has approved its first dengue vaccine, which is designed to reduce the severity of dengue infection rather than prevent mosquito bites. The news article, published on July 21, 2026, by Onmanorama, highlights the vaccine's introduction and its potential impact on public health in Kerala. The report does not provide specific case counts, death counts, or exposure numbers, nor does it mention any explicit risk level. The vaccine's approval is an official response, but there is no information on its current implementation status or ongoing investigations related to dengue. The article focuses on the vaccine's development and its role in managing dengue, without detailing the broader public health implications or the extent of its use in the region.

B 常规重要公共卫生新闻

从反应到预防:重新思考印度城市的登革热控制

From reaction to prevention: Rethinking dengue control in urban India
新闻简报一位公共卫生专家呼吁印度控制登革热的方法发生根本性转变,从被动的 暴发 管理转向持续预防。该评论于 2026 年 7 月 22 日发表,强调登革热已在该国成为高度流行病,国家 媒介 传染病控制中心报告到 2023 年 9 月已有 94,198 例病例。作者认为,城市密度的增加和气候变化正在加剧蚊子繁殖风险,因此有必要放弃季节性干预措施。拟议的战略强调综合 媒介 管理、环境管理和科学创新,以更早地破坏蚊子的生命周期。此外,这篇文章强调了社区参与和负责任的水管理在维护清洁城市环境方面的关键作用。作者建议,通过培养日常习惯和共同承担责任,印度可以增强抵御疾病的能力,并减轻公共卫生系统的长期负担。
查看新闻五要素
时间
发布于 2026 年 7 月 22 日;参考2023年9月17日的历史数据。
地点与对象
印度;特别是面临登革热风险的城市环境和社区。
事件
Subrata Pal 发表的一篇观点文章和专家评论,主张印度从被动的季节性登革热控制战略转向持续、综合的预防方法。
规模、影响与风险
引用国家 媒介 传染病控制中心 (NCVBDC) 的报告,截至 2023 年 9 月 17 日,印度记录了 94,198 例登革热感染病例;指出登革热已成为高度流行病,并且由于气候变化和城市密度,蚊子繁殖的风险正在上升。
应对、状态与不确定性
文章建议加强应对措施,包括综合 媒介 管理、环境行动、科学创新和社区参与,以降低风险;它强调,目前的努力往往是不一致的、被动的,而不是预防性的。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

From reaction to prevention: Rethinking dengue control in urban India

News Brief
A public health expert has called for a fundamental shift in India's approach to dengue control, moving from reactive outbreak management to sustained prevention. Published on July 22, 2026, the commentary highlights that dengue has become hyper-endemic in the country, with the National Center for Vector Borne Diseases Control reporting 94,198 cases by September 2023. The author argues that increasing urban density and climate change are exacerbating mosquito breeding risks, necessitating a move away from seasonal interventions. The proposed strategy emphasizes integrated vector management, environmental stewardship, and scientific innovation to disrupt the mosquito life cycle earlier. Furthermore, the piece stresses the critical role of community participation and responsible water management in maintaining cleaner urban environments. By fostering daily habits and shared responsibility, the author suggests India can build greater resilience against the disease and reduce the long-term burden on its public health system.
Original source text (English)

Series Indian Stents: Get updated Season 1 Season 2 Dx Summit Healthcare Beats UnicornNext Full Session Playlists Digital Issue Radiology Magazine Diagnostics Magazine Specials In Imaging Downloads Whitepaper HomeNewsFrom reaction to prevention: Rethinking dengue control in urban India From reaction to prevention: Rethinking dengue control in urban India Subrata Pal, Head – Fumigant Business & Public Health Business, UPL SAS, highlights why India must shift from seasonal dengue response to sustained prevention through integrated vector management, environmental action and community participation NewsGuest Blogs By Subrata Pal, Head - Fumigant Business Public Health Business, UPL SAS On Jul 22, 2026 Subrata Pal, Head - Fumigant Business & Public Health Business, UPL SAS 0 24 Share India’s public health landscape has evolved significantly over the years. Today, much greater emphasis is placed on preparing for health risks rather than merely reacting to them. However, efforts against vector-borne diseases like dengue and fever continues to be inconsistent with this idea prevention efforts tend to be spontaneous and responsive rather than continuing and preventative. Globally, mosquito-borne diseases kill more than one million people and infect up to 700 million each. Dengue has become hyper-endemic in the country. According to the report by National Center for Vector Borne Diseases Control (NCVBDC), as of September 17, 2023, 94,198 cases of dengue infection were reported in India. Mosquito breeding risks are rising each year, driven by climate change impacts and increasing urban density, which together are disrupting rainfall patterns and intensifying flooding. Despite increased awareness and coordinated mosquito control by public health agencies, prevention often focuses on peak transmission seasons. This raises an important question: can dengue prevention become less reactive and more preventive? Moving the conversation upstream Dengue management has traditionally focused on reducing adult mosquito populations once they become visible or disease incidence begins to rise. While these interventions continue to play an important role, increasing attention is being directed towards addressing the challenge earlier in the vector life cycle. This approach recognises a critical reality: stopping mosquito breeding early offers a more effective and sustainable defence against disease transmission. Today’s urban environment presents multiple conditions that support mosquito proliferation. Stagnant water in construction zones, rooftops, coolers, household containers, drains and neglected community spaces quickly become breeding habitats. These factors, combined with increasing population density and changing environmental conditions, highlight the need for earlier and more consistent intervention. From outbreak response to preventive action Building a stronger resilience against dengue requires multi-pronged approach. With changes occurring in the way that the disease spreads, there are increasing dependencies on environmental management, collective public awareness and innovation through science to assist in the prevention of dengue. Effective prevention is becoming more dependent on the collaboration of scientific innovation, environmental management, and public awareness as disease patterns continue to change. This logic has also influenced a shift to more integrated vector management solutions that are focused not only on addressing outbreaks but also on the mitigation of risk at its source. Beyond traditional prevention methods, there is a growing emphasis on new strategies to reduce mosquito populations earlier in their development cycle. Examples include naturally derived active ingredients from fermentation-based methods and a broader trend towards more targeted public health practices. However, creating lasting impact requires more than just innovation; it needs responsible water management practices and continued public participation to build healthier communities. Cleaner surroundings remain the first line of defence In addition to scientific innovation that is creating new ways to prevent disease, our daily actions are equally important. Routine activities, whether it’s cleaning up stagnant water, improving storage of drinking water, improving waste management, or routinely inspecting public places are part of the foundation of effective dengue control. The shared spaces we all use – our residential communities, schools, workplaces, governments and homes – all have a role in limiting the factors that allow mosquitoes to breed. Shifting from seasonal prevention to an ongoing public health effort, driven by awareness, accountability and daily habits, can help build a healthier environment. Building cities that prevent, not simply respond As India continues to grow and develop, there is a need to rethink how we design and maintain cleaner, healthier urban environments through a stronger public health lens, particularly in the context of dengue prevention. Achieving long-term risk reduction will require us to move beyond reactive outbreak control to a more proactive, sustainable approach. This includes creating cleaner environments and fostering community stewardship to ensure ongoing efforts to eliminate mosquito breeding grounds. Ultimately, success will not be determined only by how effectively we respond to dengue outbreaks, but by how consistently and comprehensively we remove the conditions that enable mosquitoes to thrive. We must create cities that prevent rather than respond. This requires making prevention part of our daily habits through the lens of increased awareness, shared responsibility and the consistent actions of all citizens. - Advertisement - dengue controldengue prevention Indiamosquito controlurban public healthvector borne diseases 0 24 Share Subrata Pal, Head - Fumigant Business Public Health Business, UPL SAS Prev Post NCW forms three expert groups to review ethics, regulation and medical safeguards in ART and IVF services Next Post Why the country needs a national birth anomalies registry You might also like More from author News World Brain Day 2026: Timely detection and equal access key to better brain health News Beyond survival: Why neurorehab technologies are becoming a strategic priority for... B routine priorityPublic-health news

First locally transmitted dengue case confirmed in southern France - Riviera Radio

News Brief
French health authorities have confirmed the first locally transmitted case of dengue fever this year in southern France. The case was identified in the town of Carmaux, located in the Tarn department. In response to the detection, local mosquito control measures and health investigations have been initiated to prevent further spread of the virus. This event marks the initial instance of local dengue transmission in France for the current year.

B 常规重要公共卫生新闻

大多数登革热病例与 DENV-2 毒株有关

Majority Dengue Cases Linked To DENV-2 Strain
新闻简报斯里兰卡国家登革热控制单位报告称,今年迄今为止记录的登革热病例中约 88% 是由 DENV-2 菌株引起的。该部门主任卡皮拉·坎南加拉(Kapila Kannangara)博士警告说,感染的风险会升高,因为人们对这种特定菌株的免疫力较低。全国范围内,7 月前 18 天的病例总数已升至 75,321 例,报告感染病例 19,943 例。迄今为止,暴发 已导致 53 人死亡。卫生官员已确定全国 162 名卫生部门医务人员仍处于登革热 传播 的高风险之中。
查看新闻五要素
时间
发布于 2026 年 7 月 19 日。数据涵盖今年迄今为止报告的病例,特别是 7 月的前 18 天。
地点与对象
斯里兰卡,特别是 162 个卫生官员 (MOH) 部门被确定为高风险。
事件
国家登革热控制单位报告称,今年约 88% 的登革热病例与 DENV-2 毒株有关。卡皮拉·坎南加拉 (Kapila Kannangara) 指出,由于对该菌株的免疫力较低,因此风险增加。
规模、影响与风险
今年共报告了75,321例登革热病例,其中7月前18天报告了19,943例。已有53人死亡。由于人群对 DENV-2 毒株的免疫力较低,因此该风险被认为较高。
应对、状态与不确定性
国家登革热控制单位继续监测情况,将 162 个卫生部部门确定为高风险部门。没有报道对源体的具体限制。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

Majority Dengue Cases Linked To DENV-2 Strain

News Brief
Sri Lanka's National Dengue Control Unit has reported that approximately 88% of dengue cases recorded so far this year are caused by the DENV-2 strain. Dr. Kapila Kannangara, the unit's director, warned that the risk of infection is elevated because the population has lower immunity to this specific strain. Nationally, the total number of cases has risen to 75,321, with 19,943 infections reported in just the first 18 days of July. The outbreak has resulted in 53 fatalities to date. Health officials have identified 162 Medical Officer of Health divisions across the country as remaining at high risk for dengue transmission.
Original source text (English)
homeHomeLatestFeaturedLocalSportsBusinessWorldEnglishsearchEnglishmenuMajority Dengue Cases Linked To DENV-2 Strain by Staff Writer 19-07-2026 | 2:10 PMCOLOMBO (News 1st) - The National Dengue Control Unit says that around 88% of dengue cases reported so far this year have been caused by the Dengue Virus Type 2 (DENV-2) strain.Director of the National Dengue Control Unit, Dr. Kapila Kannangara, said that the risk of Dengue is higher as people have lower immunity against this strain.The number of dengue cases reported in Sri Lanka has increased to 75,321.A total of 19,943 cases have been reported during the first 18 days of July.53 people have died due to dengue so far.The National Dengue Control Unit says that 162 Medical Officer of Health (MOH) divisions continue to remain at high risk for dengue.Other ArticlesRavindra Wijegunaratne Given Bail Gota's Former Secretary Remanded Again Shantha Bandara's Son Detained Over Robbery Lankan Passport Hits Highest Ranking In A Decade Sri Lanka Records Best Passport Ranking Since 2019 Dr.Karunawardena Appointed as New Min.Secretary Featured NewsTikTok Hero Solving Bangkok’s Parking Nightmare Sri Lanka’s First AI-Enabled Surgery Why Is Nutritious Food Becoming a Luxury? Published on July 19, 2026. Data covers cases reported so far this year and specifically the first 18 days of July.

Scale, impact and risk
A total of 75,321 dengue cases have been reported this year, with 19,943 cases in the first 18 days of July. There have been 53 deaths. The risk is considered higher due to lower population immunity to the DENV-2 strain.
